=========================================================
PROJECT NAME:
GigCredit – Privacy-First On-Device Credit Scoring Platform for Gig Workers
=========================================================

CORE IDEA:
Gig workers often lack CIBIL score and salary slips.

GigCredit uses real financial behavior:
- UPI usage
- Bank statements
- Bills
- Work proof
- Insurance
- Government schemes
- ITR / GST
- EMI obligations

System converts these into an alternative credit score (300–900).

Most processing happens ON DEVICE.

After report generation, user can apply for loans INSIDE the app.

Loan form opens inside GigCredit app (not redirect).

User submits application.

Application + score + certificate + analysis is securely sent to selected NBFC / FinTech company.

Lender reviews and gives loan.

?         GIGCREDIT � FINAL COMPLETE FEATURE ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION       ?
?         v2.0 CONSOLIDATED | All Patches Applied | Zero Gaps Guaranteed     ?
?         8 Pillars | 95 Features | All Weights Verified to Sum = 1.00       ?

?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
SECTION 0 � GLOBAL CONVENTIONS
?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????

FEATURE STANDARDS:
  � All features: normalised Float32 in range [0.0, 1.0]
  � 0.0 = worst possible signal | 1.0 = best possible signal
  � NaN / Infinity ? replaced with FALLBACK before array assembly
  � FALLBACK default = 0.40 unless explicitly stated otherwise

GLOBAL ANCHOR:
  ANCHOR = state_median_monthly_income (?)
  Source  = Aadhaar State ? RBI state median income lookup table
  Fallback = ?15,000/month (national median)

HARD SCORING BLOCKS (evaluate BEFORE any feature computation):
  � aadhaar_verified = false         ? BLOCK: "Identity not verified"
  � face_similarity_score < 0.70     ? BLOCK: "Face match failed"
  � bank_statement_absent = true     ? BLOCK: "Bank statement required"

PILLAR WEIGHT TABLE:
  P1 Income Stability        0.25
  P2 Payment Discipline      0.20
  P3 Debt Management         0.15
  P4 Savings Behaviour       0.15
  P5 Work & Identity         0.10
  P6 Financial Resilience    0.07
  P7 Social Accountability   0.05
  P8 Tax Compliance          0.03
  TOTAL                      1.00 ?

FINAL SCORE:
  raw_score = ? (pillar_score[i] � pillar_weight[i])
  gigcredit_score = round(300 + (raw_score � 600))
  Range: 300 (floor) ? 900 (ceiling)

WORK-TYPE FEATURE ROUTING:
  Inapplicable features for a work type ? weight redistributed
  proportionally across remaining applicable features in same pillar
  such that intra-pillar weights always sum to 1.00 at runtime.

BLOCKED DATA (NEVER extracted, NEVER enters any feature):
  � Social Category (Udyam field) ? BLOCKED for fairness compliance
  � Client Names in Upwork transactions ? BLOCKED for privacy


?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
SECTION 1 � UPI TRANSACTION STATEMENT SPEC
?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????

STEP: 3 (optional upload alongside bank statement)
PLATFORMS: Google Pay, PhonePe, Paytm, BHIM UPI
FORMAT: PDF export or CSV export
MANDATORY: NO � optional but provides significant booster across 6 pillars

FIELDS TO EXTRACT:
  Field                     | Type           | Purpose
  --------------------------|----------------|----------------------------
  Mobile Number             | String 10-digit| Cross-check with Step 1
  UPI ID                    | String         | Platform identity
  Statement Period From     | Date           | Coverage validation
  Statement Period To       | Date           | Coverage validation
  Transaction Date (per row)| Date           | Pattern analysis
  Transaction Type (per row)| SENT/RECEIVED  | Flow direction
  Amount (per row)          | Float          | Income/payment signal
  Counterparty Name/UPI ID  | String         | Merchant categorisation
  Transaction Narration     | String         | Description tagging
  Transaction Status        | String         | SUCCESS/FAILED/PENDING
  Transaction Reference     | String         | Deduplication only

VALIDATION RULES:
  UPI Mobile Number must exactly match Step 1 mobile number.
  Mismatch ? UPI statement rejected entirely.
  Statement period must overlap bank statement period by ? 3 months.

UPI TRANSACTION TAG ENGINE:
  Tag                   | Detection Rule
  ----------------------|---------------------------------------------------
  UPI_GIG_INCOME        | RECEIVED from: SWIGGY, ZOMATO, OLA, UBER, RAPIDO,
                        | DUNZO, BLINKIT, URBAN COMPANY, PORTER, AMAZON FLEX
  UPI_FREELANCE_INCOME  | RECEIVED from: PAYONEER, WISE, UPWORK, FIVERR
  UPI_UTILITY_PAYMENT   | SENT to: BESCOM, TNEB, TATA POWER, AIRTEL, JIO,
                        | BSNL, INDANE, HPCL, BHARAT GAS
  UPI_EMI_PAYMENT       | SENT with: EMI, LOAN, BAJAJ, HDFC LOAN, ICICI EMI
  UPI_P2P_RECEIVED      | RECEIVED from personal UPI handle (non-business)
  UPI_P2P_SENT          | SENT to personal UPI handle
  UPI_FAILED            | Any transaction where Status = FAILED or PENDING


?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
SECTION 2 � GLOBAL PREPROCESSING (compute before any pillar features)
?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????

BANK STATEMENT MONTHLY AGGREGATES:
  monthly_credit[m]         = sum(Credit Amount) for month m
  monthly_debit[m]          = sum(Debit Amount) for month m
  monthly_balance[m]        = last Running Balance of month m
  monthly_gig_credit[m]     = sum(Credit, tag=GIG_INCOME, month=m)
  monthly_emi_debit[m]      = sum(Debit, tag=EMI_DEBIT, month=m)
  monthly_utility_debit[m]  = sum(Debit, tag=UTILITY_DEBIT, month=m)
  months_covered            = count(distinct calendar months)
  REQUIRE: months_covered ? 6

INCOME SERIES:
  income_series             = [monthly_credit[1] � monthly_credit[n]]
  avg_monthly_income        = mean(income_series)
  std_monthly_income        = std(income_series)
  min_monthly_income        = min(income_series)
  max_monthly_income        = max(income_series)
  avg_closing_balance       = mean(monthly_balance[1..n])

UPI MONTHLY AGGREGATES (if statement available):
  upi_sent[m]               = sum(Amount, SENT, SUCCESS, month=m)
  upi_received[m]           = sum(Amount, RECEIVED, SUCCESS, month=m)
  upi_failed_count[m]       = count(transactions, FAILED, month=m)
  upi_total_count[m]        = count(transactions, SUCCESS, month=m)
  upi_utility_paid[m]       = sum(Amount, tag=UPI_UTILITY_PAYMENT, month=m)
  upi_gig_received[m]       = sum(Amount, tag=UPI_GIG_INCOME, month=m)

BOUNCE DETECTION (pre-label, used by F-P2-13):
  bounce_events             = count(bank rows where Description matches:
                              BOUNCE | BOUNCED | CHQ RETURN | CHQ RTN |
                              ECS RETURN | NACH RETURN | ACH RETURN |
                              INWARD RTN | OUTWARD RTN)

ATM DETECTION (pre-label, used by F-P2-15):
  atm_debit_count           = count(debit rows where Mode=ATM OR
                              Description matches: ATM|CASH WDL|WITHDRAWAL)
  total_debit_count         = count(all debit transaction rows)

SAVINGS CREDIT DETECTION (pre-label, used by F-P4-11):
  savings_debit_months      = count(months where any transaction tagged
                              SAVINGS_CREDIT with Debit > 0 exists)
  Tag SAVINGS_CREDIT when Description matches:
    SIP | RD | RECURRING DEPOSIT | PPF | MUTUAL FUND | ELSS

EMI DETECTION (pre-label, used by P2, P3):
  emi_rows                  = bank rows where Description matches:
                              EMI | LOAN | INSTALLMENT | NACH EMI | ECS EMI
  Tag: EMI_DEBIT

UTILITY MERGE (bank + UPI combined):
  For each utility bill month m:
    bank_utility_match[m]   = 1 if bank has matching debit else 0
    upi_utility_match[m]    = 1 if UPI has matching UPI_UTILITY_PAYMENT else 0
    utility_match[m]        = max(bank_match[m], upi_match[m])

FX CONVERSION:
  For international platform income (USD):
    amount_inr = amount_usd � RBI_reference_rate on payout date
    FX fallback = 83.0 (if RBI rate unavailable)
  Platform credits from PAYONEER / WISE / UPWORK PAYMENT tagged as
  UPI_FREELANCE_INCOME in UPI and GIG_INCOME in bank.

PILLAR 1 � INCOME STABILITY
Pillar Weight: 0.25 | Feature Count: 13 | All work types applicable

P1 INTRA-PILLAR WEIGHTS:
  F-P1-01: 0.15  F-P1-02: 0.14  F-P1-03: 0.09  F-P1-04: 0.07
  F-P1-05: 0.09  F-P1-06: 0.10  F-P1-07: 0.10  F-P1-08: 0.05

????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
F-P1-01 | avg_monthly_income_norm
????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
MEASURES   : Absolute income level vs state median
SOURCE     : Bank Statement ? monthly_credit series | ANCHOR
FORMULA    : avg_monthly_income � (ANCHOR � 2.5) | Clamp [0.0, 1.0]
EXAMPLE    : avg=?18,000, ANCHOR=?15,000 ? 18000�37500 = 0.48
FALLBACK   : 0.40
WEIGHT     : 0.15

????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
F-P1-02 | income_stability_cv
????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
MEASURES   : Month-to-month income consistency (lower variance = better)
SOURCE     : Bank Statement ? monthly_credit series
FORMULA    : cv = std_monthly_income � avg_monthly_income
             income_stability_cv = 1.0 ? min(cv, 1.0)
EXAMPLE    : avg=?18,000, std=?3,600 ? cv=0.20 ? score=0.80
FALLBACK   : 0.40
WEIGHT     : 0.14

????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
F-P1-03 | income_growth_slope
????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
MEASURES   : Whether income is trending up, flat, or declining
SOURCE     : Bank Statement ? monthly_credit series
FORMULA    : slope = linear_regression_slope(income_series)
             slope_norm = (slope � avg_monthly_income) + 0.5
             Clamp [0.0, 1.0]
EXAMPLE    : +?500/month on ?20,000 base ? 0.025+0.5 = 0.525
FALLBACK   : 0.50 (flat income assumed)
WEIGHT     : 0.09

????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
F-P1-04 | income_source_diversity
????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
MEASURES   : Number of distinct income source categories in bank
SOURCE     : Bank Statement ? tagged income categories
FORMULA    : distinct_tags = count(unique tags in {GIG_INCOME,

????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????

             LPG Bills ? Order Status (completed = on-time always)

               on_time[i] = 1 if Payment Date ? Due Date else 0
             utility_ontime_ratio = sum(on_time) � count(bills with Due Date)
             LPG: Order Status = "Completed" ? on_time = 1 (cash/immediate)
             Bills with missing Due Date ? excluded from denominator entirely
             (neither counted as late nor on-time)

             EMI Screenshot ? EMI Duration + Monthly EMI Amount
FORMULA    : estimated_outstanding = Monthly EMI � remaining months
             total_outstanding = sum(estimated_outstanding across all loans)
             annual_income = avg_monthly_income � 12
             debt_to_income = total_outstanding � annual_income
             total_outstanding_to_income:
               debt_to_income ? 0.5 ? 1.00
               debt_to_income ? 1.0 ? 0.75
               debt_to_income ? 2.0 ? 0.50
               debt_to_income ? 3.0 ? 0.25
               debt_to_income > 3.0 ? 0.05
             No loans: return 1.00
FALLBACK   : 0.60
WEIGHT     : 0.06

               > avg_monthly_income � 0.30)

  itr_ontime_filing_binary � computed ONCE.
  Index 22 (F-P2-10) and Index 90 (F-P8-03) hold the SAME value.
  Compute during P2 preprocessing, copy value to P8 slot.
  Do NOT recompute.


?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
SECTION 4 � RUNTIME WEIGHT REDISTRIBUTION ENGINE
?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????

PURPOSE:
  Work-type-restricted features must have their weights redistributed
  at runtime so intra-pillar weights always sum to 1.00 regardless of
  which features are applicable for the user's work type.

REDISTRIBUTION ALGORITHM:
  For each pillar P:
    1. Build list: applicable_features = all features in P where
       work_type restriction matches user's work_type OR feature
       has no restriction (applies to ALL work types)
    2. removed_weight = sum(weights of non-applicable features)
    3. For each applicable feature f:
       redistributed_weight[f] = base_weight[f] +
         (base_weight[f] � sum_of_applicable_base_weights) � removed_weight
    4. Verify: sum(redistributed_weight) == 1.00 (assert before scoring)

WORK TYPE RESTRICTION SUMMARY:
  Feature              | Restricted To
  ---------------------|----------------------------------
  F-P1-09              | Vendors, Freelancers (GST users)
  F-P1-10              | Vendors / Sellers only
  F-P4-10              | Vendors / Tradespeople only
  F-P5-04              | Platform Workers + Freelancers
  F-P5-06              | Platform Workers + Freelancers
  F-P5-08              | Platform Workers + Freelancers
  F-P5-09              | Vendors / Tradespeople only
  F-P5-10              | Tradespeople (primary); all accepted
  F-P5-11              | Platform Workers + Freelancers
  F-P5-12              | Platform Workers (vehicle-based)
  F-P5-13              | Vendors / Tradespeople only
  F-P5-15              | Vendors / Tradespeople only
  F-P5-16              | Freelancers only
  F-P5-17              | Platform Workers only
  F-P6-07              | Vehicle Workers only
  F-P6-08              | Vehicle Workers only

REDISTRIBUTION EXAMPLE:
  User Work Type: Freelancer
  P5 features F-P5-09 (vendors), F-P5-12 (vehicle), F-P5-13 (vendors),
  F-P5-15 (trade licences), F-P5-17 (platform type) ? NOT applicable.
  Combined removed weight = 0.04+0.04+0.03+0.02+0.02 = 0.15
  Remaining 13 features get proportional boost to fill the 0.15 gap.
  Final sum of redistributed weights for applicable features = 1.00 ?


?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????
SECTION 5 � FINAL SCORING ENGINE
?????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????

STEP 1 � PRE-SCORE HARD BLOCK CHECKS:
  IF aadhaar_verified = false      ? RETURN ERROR "Identity not verified"
  IF face_similarity_score < 0.70  ? RETURN ERROR "Face match failed"
  IF bank_statement_absent = true  ? RETURN ERROR "Bank statement required"
  Only proceed to Step 2 if ALL three checks pass.

STEP 2 � PREPROCESSING:
  Execute all Section 2 global preprocessing computations.
  Populate pre-computed variables:
    income_series, monthly_balance, bounce_events,
    atm_debit_count, savings_debit_months, utility_match,
    upi monthly aggregates (if UPI submitted), FX rates.

STEP 3 � COMPUTE ALL 95 FEATURES:
  Iterate feature vector indices 0�94 in order.
  For each feature:
    a. Apply FALLBACK if source document absent or OCR confidence < 0.85
    b. Compute formula as specified
    c. Clamp result to [0.0, 1.0]
    d. Apply work-type restriction: if not applicable ? use FALLBACK
       (weight will be redistributed, not zeroed out)
    e. Store result in feature_vector[idx]
  Apply F-P2-07 modifier (premium frequency adjustment) after F-P2-07 computed.

STEP 4 � APPLY RUNTIME WEIGHT REDISTRIBUTION (Section 4 algorithm):
  For each pillar independently.
  Produce redistributed_weights[pillar][feature].
  Assert sum = 1.00 per pillar.

STEP 5 � COMPUTE PILLAR SCORES:
  For each pillar P ? {P1, P2, P3, P4, P5, P6, P7, P8}:
    pillar_score[P] = ? (feature_vector[f] � redistributed_weight[P][f])
                      for all applicable features f in pillar P
  Each pillar_score ? [0.0, 1.0]

STEP 6 � COMPUTE RAW SCORE:
  raw_score = (pillar_score[P1] � 0.25)
            + (pillar_score[P2] � 0.20)
            + (pillar_score[P3] � 0.15)
            + (pillar_score[P4] � 0.15)
            + (pillar_score[P5] � 0.10)
            + (pillar_score[P6] � 0.07)
            + (pillar_score[P7] � 0.05)
            + (pillar_score[P8] � 0.03)
  raw_score ? [0.0, 1.0]

STEP 7 � APPLY DOCUMENT CONFIDENCE MULTIPLIER:
  submitted_steps = count(steps where at least one document was
    successfully verified AND OCR confidence ? 0.85)
  Max possible steps = 8
  confidence = submitted_steps � 8
  IF confidence < 0.50:
    confidence_penalty = 0.85   (15% penalty for sparse submissions)
  ELSE:
    confidence_penalty = 1.00   (no penalty)
  adjusted_score = raw_score � confidence_penalty
  Clamp adjusted_score to [0.0, 1.0]

STEP 8 � MAP TO GIGCREDIT SCALE:
  gigcredit_score = round(300 + (adjusted_score � 600))
  Minimum possible: 300 | Maximum possible: 900

STEP 9 � ASSIGN RISK BAND:
  Score 750�900 ? BAND: LOW RISK        ? "Recommended for approval"
  Score 600�749 ? BAND: MEDIUM RISK     ? "Approve with standard terms"
  Score 500�599 ? BAND: MODERATE RISK   ? "Approve with enhanced monitoring"
  Score 400�499 ? BAND: HIGH RISK       ? "Approve with collateral or guarantor"
  Score 300�399 ? BAND: VERY HIGH RISK  ? "Decline or defer"

STEP 10 � SCORE EXPLAINABILITY OUTPUT:
  For each pillar, emit:
    {
      pillar_name:      string,
      pillar_score:     float (0.0�1.0),
      pillar_weight:    float,
      weighted_contrib: float (pillar_score � pillar_weight),
      top_3_features:   [feature_name, value, weight] sorted by
                         (weight � abs(value ? 0.5)) descending
    }
  Top 3 positive drivers  = features where value > 0.70, sorted by weighted impact
  Top 3 negative drivers  = features where value < 0.40, sorted by weighted impact
  These are returned in the API response for lender dashboard display.

STEP 11 � FAIRNESS AUDIT LOG (mandatory):
  Log separately (NOT in score computation):
    age_risk_band value + age_years
    eshram_occupation_match_binary value
    gender (profile metadata only � NOT a feature, NOT in vector)
  Flag if age_risk_band is the sole reason for score < 400.
  This log is written to a separate audit collection in MongoDB.
  It must never influence re-scoring.

--- specification folders_new\GIGCREDIT-BACKEND-SERVER-SPECIFICATION.txt ---

================================================================================
GIGCREDIT — BACKEND SERVER SPECIFICATION
VERSION 1.0 FINAL
SCOPE: Verification API Layer + LLM Report Generation Layer
AUDIENCE: Backend Developer / Team Collaboration Reference
================================================================================

THIS DOCUMENT COVERS:
  - What the backend server is and is NOT responsible for
  - Clear boundary between on-device and backend
  - Full verification API flow (Step 1 through Step 7)
  - Full LLM report generation flow (SHAP → Groq → Plain English)
  - Request/response schemas
  - Error handling and fallback rules
  - Security and privacy rules
  - Performance targets

THIS DOCUMENT DOES NOT COVER:
  - ML model training (see ML Specification document)
  - Feature engineering (see Feature Engineering Specification)
  - On-device scoring logic (see Scoring Engine Specification)
  - SHAP computation (see SHAP Specification)
  - Flutter UI rendering (see Frontend Specification)
  - Final report assembly and PDF export (see Report Pipeline Specification)

================================================================================
SECTION 0 — SYSTEM PHILOSOPHY
================================================================================

GigCredit is a PRIVACY-FIRST, ON-DEVICE credit scoring platform.

The scoring engine runs entirely on the user's Flutter device.
The backend server exists for two purposes ONLY:

  PURPOSE 1 — VERIFICATION
    Verify that user-provided identifiers (Aadhaar, PAN, RC, Insurance, etc.)
    match real records in a simulated government/financial database.

  PURPOSE 2 — LANGUAGE CONVERSION
    Convert structured SHAP output (machine data) into plain English or
    regional language explanation text and personalized suggestions
    using the Groq LLM API.

THE BACKEND NEVER:
  - Computes credit scores
  - Runs ML models
  - Performs feature engineering
  - Processes bank statements
  - Reads raw OCR output
  - Stores user documents
  - Stores Aadhaar / PAN / bank transaction data

THE BACKEND ONLY RECEIVES:
  - Minimal identifiers for verification (e.g., PAN number only)
  - Structured score + SHAP explanation JSON for LLM processing

================================================================================
SECTION 1 — ARCHITECTURE BOUNDARY: ON-DEVICE vs BACKEND
================================================================================

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      USER'S FLUTTER DEVICE                      │
│                                                                 │
│  ┌─────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌─────────────────────┐  │
│  │  Document   │   │  On-Device   │   │   Scoring Engine    │  │
│  │  Capture    │──▶│  OCR Engine  │──▶│   (m2cgen Dart)     │  │
│  │  (Camera)   │   │  (PaddleOCR) │   │   7 Pillars         │  │
│  └─────────────┘   └──────────────┘   └────────┬────────────┘  │
│                                                │               │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────▼────────────┐  │
│  │              Feature Engineering (Dart)                  │  │
│  │              95 Features → Normalized 0.0–1.0            │  │
│  └──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘  │
│                                 │                               │
│  ┌──────────────────────────────▼───────────────────────────┐  │
│  │              SHAP Lookup Engine (Dart)                   │  │
│  │              shap_lookup.json → Top 3 Pos + Top 3 Neg    │  │
│  └──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘  │
│                                 │                               │
│  ┌──────────────────────────────▼───────────────────────────┐  │
│  │         Explanation Payload Builder (Dart)               │  │
│  │         Assembles structured JSON for LLM                │  │
│  └──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘  │
└────────────────────────────────-│───────────────────────────────┘

                    ┌─────────────▼─────────────┐
                    │   HTTPS (TLS + HMAC Auth)  │
                    └─────────────┬─────────────┘

│                     (FastAPI + MongoDB)                         │

│  │  Verification API Layer  │   │  LLM Report Layer         │  │
│  │                          │   │                           │  │
│  │  /gov/aadhaar/verify     │   │  /api/report/generate     │  │
│  │  /gov/pan/verify         │   │        │                  │  │
│  │  /bank/ifsc/verify       │   │        ▼                  │  │
│  │  /bank/account/verify    │   │  Prompt Builder           │  │
│  │  /bank/loan/check        │   │        │                  │  │
│  │  /gov/vehicle/rc/verify  │   │        ▼                  │  │
│  │  /gov/eshram/verify      │   │  Groq API Call            │  │
│  │  /gov/pmsym/verify       │   │  (llama3-70b-8192)        │  │
│  │  /gov/insurance/verify   │   │        │                  │  │
│  │  /auth/otp/send          │   │        ▼                  │  │
│  │  /auth/otp/verify        │   │  Response Formatter       │  │
│  │                          │   │        │                  │  │
│  │  MongoDB Query Layer     │   │        ▼                  │  │
│  │  (Simulated DB)          │   │  JSON Response to App     │  │
│  └──────────────────────────┘   └───────────────────────────┘  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                  │
                    ┌─────────────▼─────────────┐
                    │   Response returns to App  │
                    └─────────────┬─────────────┘
                                  │
┌─────────────────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────┐
│                      USER'S FLUTTER DEVICE                      │
│                                                                 │
│  Verification Response → On-Device Cross-Validation             │
│  LLM Response → Final Report Rendering (UI + PDF)              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

KEY RULE:
  The backend returns data to the app.
  The app does all final processing, cross-validation, and rendering.
  The backend never makes decisions about the user's creditworthiness.

================================================================================
SECTION 2 — TECHNOLOGY STACK
================================================================================

Backend Framework  : FastAPI (Python 3.11+)
Database           : MongoDB (local for development, Atlas for production)
LLM Provider       : Groq API (model: llama3-70b-8192)
Authentication     : HMAC-SHA256 request signing + API Key header
Transport Security : HTTPS (TLS 1.3)
Rate Limiting      : Per-device, per-endpoint throttling
Deployment         : Docker container (single service)

Environment Variables Required:
  GROQ_API_KEY         = <your Groq API key>
  MONGODB_URI          = <MongoDB connection string>
  HMAC_SECRET          = <shared secret with Flutter app>
  SERVER_API_KEY       = <static API key for app requests>

================================================================================
SECTION 3 — REQUEST AUTHENTICATION
================================================================================

Every request from the Flutter app to the backend must include:

  Header: X-Api-Key: <SERVER_API_KEY>
  Header: X-Device-Id: <hashed device fingerprint>
  Header: X-Timestamp: <Unix timestamp>
  Header: X-Signature: <HMAC-SHA256 signature>

Signature Computation (on-device):
  body_hash  = SHA256(raw_request_body)
  message    = device_id + ":" + timestamp + ":" + body_hash
  signature  = HMAC-SHA256(HMAC_SECRET, message)

Server Validation:
  1. Check X-Api-Key matches SERVER_API_KEY
  2. Check X-Timestamp is within ±5 minutes (replay protection)
  3. Recompute HMAC and compare with X-Signature
  4. If any check fails → return 401 Unauthorized

All requests rejected if:
  - Missing any header
  - Timestamp expired (> 5 min old)
  - HMAC mismatch
  - API key invalid

================================================================================
SECTION 4 — VERIFICATION API LAYER
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
  The app sends a minimal identifier extracted on-device via OCR or user input.
  The server queries MongoDB (simulated government/bank database).
  The server returns the matching record fields.
  The app compares returned data against OCR-extracted data on-device.

CRITICAL RULE:
  The server ONLY matches identifiers.
  The app decides if the document is verified or not.
  The server never sees full documents, images, or transaction history.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.1 OTP FLOW (Step 1 — Mobile Verification)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /auth/otp/send

  Purpose   : Send OTP to user's mobile number
  Trigger   : User enters mobile number in Step 1
  App Sends : { "mobile": "9876543210" }

  Server Logic:
    1. Validate mobile is 10-digit numeric
    2. Generate 6-digit OTP
    3. Store OTP in otp_db with expiry = 5 minutes
    4. Simulate OTP send (for demo: return OTP in response)
    5. In production: integrate SMS gateway

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "sent",
      "expires_in_seconds": 300
    }

  Error Cases:
    Invalid mobile format → 400 { "error": "invalid_mobile" }
    Rate limit exceeded   → 429 { "error": "too_many_requests" }

---

ENDPOINT: POST /auth/otp/verify

  Purpose   : Verify OTP entered by user
  App Sends : { "mobile": "9876543210", "otp": "482917" }

  Server Logic:
    1. Query otp_db for matching mobile
    2. Check OTP matches and is not expired
    3. Mark mobile as verified in session

  Response (200 OK):
    { "status": "verified", "mobile_verified": true }

  Error Cases:
    OTP mismatch     → 400 { "error": "invalid_otp" }
    OTP expired      → 400 { "error": "otp_expired" }
    Mobile not found → 404 { "error": "not_found" }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.2 AADHAAR VERIFICATION (Step 2)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /gov/aadhaar/verify

  Purpose   : Verify Aadhaar number exists and is active
  Trigger   : After on-device OCR extracts Aadhaar number from card image
  App Sends : { "aadhaar": "123456789012" }

  Server Logic:
    1. Query aadhaar_db where aadhaar == input
    2. If found → return record fields
    3. If not found → return invalid

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "valid",
      "name": "Ravi Kumar",
      "dob": "1997-06-12",
      "state": "Tamil Nadu"
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    Compare API name vs OCR name from Aadhaar front image
    If match (fuzzy ≥ 85%) → aadhaar_verified = true
    If mismatch → flag for re-upload, aadhaar_verified = false

  Error Cases:
    Aadhaar not found → 404 { "status": "invalid", "error": "not_found" }
    Invalid format    → 400 { "error": "invalid_format" }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.3 PAN VERIFICATION (Step 2)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /gov/pan/verify

  Purpose   : Verify PAN number exists and is active
  Trigger   : After on-device OCR extracts PAN from card image
  App Sends : { "pan": "ABCDE1234F" }

  Server Logic:
    1. Validate PAN format regex [A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1}
    2. Query pan_db where pan == input
    3. Return matching record

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "valid",
      "name": "Ravi Kumar",
      "dob": "1997-06-12",
      "pan_active": true,
      "itr_filed": true,
      "itr_years": [2022, 2023, 2024]
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    Compare API name vs OCR name from PAN image
    If match → pan_verified = true
    itr_filed and itr_years stored in VerifiedProfile for P6 feature

  Error Cases:
    PAN not found  → 404 { "status": "invalid", "error": "not_found" }
    PAN inactive   → 200 { "status": "inactive", "pan_active": false }
    Invalid format → 400 { "error": "invalid_format" }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.4 BANK IFSC VERIFICATION (Step 3)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /bank/ifsc/verify

  Purpose   : Verify IFSC code is valid and get bank branch details
  App Sends : { "ifsc": "HDFC0001234" }

  Server Logic:
    1. Validate IFSC format [A-Z]{4}0[A-Z0-9]{6}
    2. Query ifsc_db where ifsc == input
    3. Return bank details

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "valid",
      "bank_name": "HDFC Bank",
      "branch_name": "Anna Nagar Chennai",
      "city": "Chennai",
      "state": "Tamil Nadu"
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    Compare returned bank_name vs user-entered bank name
    If match → ifsc_verified = true

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.5 BANK ACCOUNT VERIFICATION (Step 3)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /bank/account/verify

  Purpose   : Verify bank account exists and is active
  App Sends : { "account_number": "1234567890", "ifsc": "HDFC0001234" }

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "valid",
      "account_holder": "Ravi Kumar",
      "account_type": "Savings",
      "account_active": true
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    Compare account_holder vs Aadhaar name
    If match → bank_account_verified = true

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.6 LOAN CHECK (Step 3 — Internal, Automated)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /bank/loan/check

  Purpose   : Check if user has active loans linked to their bank account
  Trigger   : Runs automatically after bank account verification
  App Sends : { "account_number": "1234567890" }

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "has_active_loans": true,
      "loan_count": 2,
      "loans": [
        { "type": "Personal Loan", "emi_amount": 3500, "remaining_months": 18 },
        { "type": "Two-Wheeler Loan", "emi_amount": 1800, "remaining_months": 6 }
      ]
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    Loan data fed into P3 (Debt Management) feature engineering
    emi_to_income_ratio computed from this data

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.7 VEHICLE RC VERIFICATION (Step 5 — Platform Workers Only)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /gov/vehicle/rc/verify

  Purpose   : Verify vehicle registration certificate
  Trigger   : Step 5A (Platform Worker only), after OCR extracts vehicle number
  App Sends : { "vehicle_number": "TN09AB1234" }

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "valid",
      "owner_name": "Ravi Kumar",
      "vehicle_class": "Motorcycle",
      "chassis_number": "ME4JC092XRM123456",
      "engine_number": "JC09E2123456",
      "registration_date": "2021-03-15",
      "rc_expiry": "2036-03-14",
      "fitness_expiry": "2027-03-14"
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    Compare owner_name vs Aadhaar name → must match
    rc_expiry must be > today
    vehicle_class stored for DL class matching

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.8 ESHRAM VERIFICATION (Step 6)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /gov/eshram/verify

  Purpose   : Verify e-Shram registration (unorganized worker registry)
  App Sends : { "uan": "UAN123456789012" }

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "registered",
      "name": "Ravi Kumar",
      "worker_category": "Gig Worker",
      "registration_date": "2022-08-10"
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    eshram_registered = true → feeds into P7 (Social Accountability) features

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.9 PM-SYM VERIFICATION (Step 6)

ENDPOINT: POST /gov/pmsym/verify

  Purpose   : Verify PM-SYM pension scheme contribution
  App Sends : { "uan": "UAN123456789012" }

  Response (200 OK):
    {
      "status": "active",
      "months_contributed": 14,
      "last_contribution_date": "2026-03-01"
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    pmsym_active = true → feeds into P7 features

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ENDPOINT: POST /gov/insurance/policy/verify

  Purpose   : Verify insurance policy (health, life, or vehicle)
  App Sends : {
                "policy_number": "HLT2024112345",
                "policy_type": "health"
              }

  Response (200 OK — Health):
    {
      "status": "active",
      "policy_holder": "Ravi Kumar",
      "insurer": "Star Health Insurance",
      "sum_insured": 500000,
      "premium_annual": 8500,
      "policy_start": "2024-11-01",
      "policy_expiry": "2025-10-31"
    }

  Response (200 OK — Vehicle):
    {
      "status": "active",
      "policy_holder": "Ravi Kumar",
      "vehicle_number": "TN09AB1234",
      "insurer": "Bajaj Allianz",
      "policy_expiry": "2026-10-15"
    }

  On-Device After Response:
    policy_expiry must be > today
    policy_holder must match Aadhaar name
    Sum insured and premium stored for P6 (Financial Resilience) features

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.11 COMPLETE VERIFICATION TIMELINE (ALL STEPS)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  STEP 1 → /auth/otp/send + /auth/otp/verify
  STEP 2 → /gov/aadhaar/verify + /gov/pan/verify
  STEP 3 → /bank/ifsc/verify + /bank/account/verify + /bank/loan/check
  STEP 4 → No backend call (utility bills processed on-device only)
  STEP 5 → /gov/vehicle/rc/verify (Platform Workers only)
  STEP 6 → /gov/eshram/verify + /gov/pmsym/verify
  STEP 7 → /gov/insurance/policy/verify (1–3 calls depending on policy types)

  TOTAL MAXIMUM API CALLS ACROSS ALL STEPS: 11 calls
  ALL CALLS ARE LIGHTWEIGHT (identifier only, no documents)
  ALL CALLS RETURN IN 50–150ms (MongoDB lookup, no heavy computation)

================================================================================
SECTION 5 — LLM REPORT GENERATION LAYER
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
  After on-device scoring completes and SHAP lookup runs, the app has a
  structured explanation (machine-readable JSON). This JSON is sent to the
  backend, which builds a prompt and calls the Groq API to convert the
  data into plain English (or regional language) human-readable text.

  The LLM returns ONLY:
    - A plain English explanation of why the user got their score
    - 3 personalized, actionable improvement suggestions

  The LLM does NOT:
    - Modify the credit score
    - Modify pillar scores
    - Store any user data
    - Make credit decisions

  The LLM output is returned to the app for display in the final report.
  The final report assembly (PDF, UI rendering) happens on-device.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.1 WHAT HAPPENS ON-DEVICE BEFORE THE LLM CALL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  STAGE 1 — SCORING (on-device):
    All 7 pillar models run sequentially in Dart:
      P1 → XGBoost scorer (m2cgen Dart)
      P2 → XGBoost scorer (m2cgen Dart)
      P3 → XGBoost scorer (m2cgen Dart)
      P4 → XGBoost scorer (m2cgen Dart)
      P5 → Dart Scorecard (weighted sum)
      P6 → RandomForest scorer (m2cgen Dart)
      P7 → Dart Scorecard (weighted sum)

    Meta-learner (Logistic Regression, pure Dart dot product):
      Input  : 7 pillar scores + work-type one-hot + interaction terms
      Output : probability (0.0–1.0)
      Score  : round(probability × 600) + 300 → range 300–900

  STAGE 2 — SHAP LOOKUP (on-device):
    App reads shap_lookup.json (pre-bundled in app at install)
    For each of the 88 features:
      - Find the feature's value bin
      - Read pre-computed SHAP impact for that bin
    Sort all features by |impact| descending
    Select:
      Top 3 positive features (highest positive SHAP impact)
      Top 3 negative features (most negative SHAP impact)

  STAGE 3 — EXPLANATION PAYLOAD BUILD (on-device):
    App assembles the structured JSON payload
    (defined in Section 5.2 below)
    This payload is the ONLY thing sent to the backend

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.2 EXPLANATION PAYLOAD SCHEMA (App → Backend)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  ENDPOINT: POST /api/report/generate

  REQUEST BODY:
  {
    "credit_score": 682,
    "grade": "B",
    "risk_level": "Medium",
    "work_type": "platform_worker",
    "language": "Tamil",
    "pillar_scores": {
      "income_stability": 72,
      "payment_discipline": 68,
      "debt_management": 55,
      "savings_behaviour": 61,
      "work_identity": 78,
      "financial_resilience": 45,
      "social_accountability": 60
    },
    "positive_factors": [
      {
        "feature_label": "Consistent monthly income",
        "pillar": "Income Stability",
        "impact": 15
      },
      {
        "feature_label": "Utility bills paid on time",
        "pillar": "Payment Discipline",
        "impact": 12
      },
      {
        "feature_label": "Bank balance growing steadily",
        "pillar": "Savings Behaviour",
        "impact": 9
      }
    ],
    "negative_factors": [
      {
        "feature_label": "EMI payments high relative to income",
        "pillar": "Debt Management",
        "impact": -18
      },
      {
        "feature_label": "No active health insurance",
        "pillar": "Financial Resilience",
        "impact": -10
      },
      {
        "feature_label": "Low monthly savings rate",
        "pillar": "Savings Behaviour",
        "impact": -7
      }
    ],
    "confidence_level": "High"
  }

  FIELD RULES:
    credit_score       : integer, 300–900
    grade              : string, one of S / A / B / C / D / E
    risk_level         : string, one of Low / Medium / High
    work_type          : string, one of platform_worker / vendor / tradesperson / freelancer
    language           : string, one of English / Hindi / Tamil / Telugu / Kannada
    pillar_scores      : object, all 7 pillars, values 0–100
    positive_factors   : array, exactly 3 items
    negative_factors   : array, exactly 3 items
    feature_label      : human-readable label (NOT raw feature name like emi_to_income_ratio)
    impact             : integer, SHAP impact magnitude (positive = positive, negative = negative)
    confidence_level   : string, one of High / Medium / Low

  WHAT IS NOT SENT:
    - Aadhaar number
    - PAN number
    - Bank account number
    - Any transaction data
    - Raw feature values (emi_to_income_ratio: 0.72 etc.)
    - Document images or OCR text

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.3 BACKEND PROMPT BUILDER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The backend receives the payload and constructs a structured prompt
  for the Groq API.

  PROMPT TEMPLATE:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ You are a friendly financial advisor explaining a gig worker's       │
  │ credit score in simple, clear language. Avoid technical jargon.     │
  │ Write as if speaking directly to the user.                          │
  │                                                                      │
  │ USER PROFILE:                                                        │
  │ Work Type    : {work_type_label}                                     │
  │ Credit Score : {credit_score} out of 900                            │
  │ Grade        : {grade}                                               │
  │ Risk Level   : {risk_level}                                          │
  │ Confidence   : {confidence_level}                                    │
  │                                                                      │
  │ SCORE BREAKDOWN:                                                     │
  │ Income Stability       : {income_stability}/100                      │
  │ Payment Discipline     : {payment_discipline}/100                    │
  │ Debt Management        : {debt_management}/100                       │
  │ Savings Behaviour      : {savings_behaviour}/100                     │
  │ Work and Identity      : {work_identity}/100                         │
  │ Financial Resilience   : {financial_resilience}/100                  │
  │ Social Accountability  : {social_accountability}/100                 │
  │                                                                      │
  │ YOUR STRENGTHS (factors boosting your score):                       │
  │ 1. {positive_1_label} (+{positive_1_impact} points)                 │
  │ 2. {positive_2_label} (+{positive_2_impact} points)                 │
  │ 3. {positive_3_label} (+{positive_3_impact} points)                 │
  │                                                                      │
  │ AREAS PULLING YOUR SCORE DOWN:                                      │
  │ 1. {negative_1_label} ({negative_1_impact} points)                  │
  │ 2. {negative_2_label} ({negative_2_impact} points)                  │
  │ 3. {negative_3_label} ({negative_3_impact} points)                  │
  │                                                                      │
  │ TASK:                                                                │
  │ 1. Write a 4–5 sentence explanation of the credit score.            │
  │    - Start with overall performance (score + grade).                │
  │    - Mention the top strengths specifically.                         │
  │    - Mention the key concerns specifically.                          │
  │    - End with an encouraging closing sentence.                       │
  │                                                                      │
  │ 2. Write exactly 3 personalized improvement suggestions.            │
  │    - Each suggestion must be specific and actionable.               │
  │    - Reference the actual concern from the negative factors.        │
  │    - Keep each suggestion to 1–2 sentences maximum.                 │
  │                                                                      │
  │ OUTPUT FORMAT (strict JSON):                                         │
  │ {                                                                    │
  │   "explanation": "<4-5 sentence paragraph>",                        │
  │   "suggestions": [                                                   │
  │     "<suggestion 1>",                                               │
  │     "<suggestion 2>",                                               │
  │     "<suggestion 3>"                                                 │
  │   ]                                                                  │
  │ }                                                                    │
  │                                                                      │
  │ LANGUAGE: Write the entire response in {language}.                  │
  │ DO NOT include any other text outside the JSON.                     │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  work_type_label mapping:
    platform_worker → "Delivery / Ride Partner"
    vendor          → "Vendor / Seller"
    tradesperson    → "Skilled Tradesperson"
    freelancer      → "Freelancer / Creative Professional"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.4 GROQ API CALL (Backend → Groq)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Provider  : Groq (https://api.groq.com)
  Model     : llama3-70b-8192
  API Key   : GROQ_API_KEY (environment variable, never exposed to app)

  GROQ REQUEST:
  {
    "model": "llama3-70b-8192",
    "messages": [
      {
        "role": "user",
        "content": "<fully constructed prompt from Section 5.3>"
      }
    ],
    "temperature": 0.4,
    "max_tokens": 600,
    "response_format": { "type": "json_object" }
  }

  PARAMETER RATIONALE:
    temperature 0.4 : Low randomness — consistent, professional tone
    max_tokens 600  : Enough for 4-5 sentence explanation + 3 suggestions
    json_object     : Forces strict JSON output, prevents free-text wrapping

  GROQ RESPONSE (raw):
  {
    "choices": [
      {
        "message": {
          "content": "{"explanation": "...", "suggestions": ["...", "...", "..."]}"
        }
      }
    ]
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.5 BACKEND RESPONSE FORMATTING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Backend parses the Groq response JSON.
  Validates that explanation and suggestions fields exist.
  Returns formatted response to app.

  ENDPOINT RESPONSE (Backend → App):
  {
    "status": "success",
    "language": "Tamil",
    "explanation": "உங்கள் கிரெடிட் ஸ்கோர் 682 ஆக உள்ளது, இது B தரத்தில் உள்ளது...",
    "suggestions": [
      "உங்கள் மாதாந்திர EMI செலுத்துதல்களை குறைக்க, அதிக வட்டி கடன்களை முதலில் அடைக்க முயற்சி செய்யுங்கள்.",
      "ஒரு உடல்நல காப்பீட்டு திட்டம் எடுங்கள் — இது உங்கள் நிதி நெகிழ்வு மதிப்பை 10+ புள்ளிகள் மேம்படுத்தும்.",
      "மாதாந்திர வருமானத்தில் குறைந்தது 10% RD அல்லது SIP மூலம் சேமியுங்கள்."
    ],
    "model_used": "llama3-70b-8192",
    "generated_at": "2026-04-24T16:43:00Z"
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5.6 WHAT HAPPENS ON-DEVICE AFTER LLM RESPONSE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The app receives the LLM response and:

  STEP 1 — Validate response:
    Check status == "success"
    Check explanation is non-empty string
    Check suggestions is array of exactly 3 items
    If validation fails → use fallback template (see Section 6)

  STEP 2 — Merge with on-device score data:
    The app already has:
      - Credit score (300–900)
      - Grade and risk band
      - 7 pillar score values
      - Top 3 positive SHAP factors
      - Top 3 negative SHAP factors
    The LLM adds:
      - explanation text (human-readable narrative)
      - suggestions array (3 actionable items)

  STEP 3 — Assemble final report object:
    FinalReport = {
      score data (on-device),
      pillar breakdown (on-device),
      SHAP factors (on-device),
      explanation (LLM via backend),
      suggestions (LLM via backend)
    }

  STEP 4 — Render on screen and PDF:
    The Flutter UI renders the final report
    User can export as PDF
    PDF generation happens entirely on-device
    NOTHING from the final report is sent back to the server

  THE LLM OUTPUT IS THE FINAL PIECE.
  THE BACKEND IS NOT INVOLVED IN FINAL REPORT ASSEMBLY.
  THE BACKEND IS NOT INVOLVED IN PDF GENERATION.

================================================================================
SECTION 6 — FALLBACK RULES
================================================================================

FALLBACK SCENARIO 1 — LLM API UNAVAILABLE (No Internet):
  App detects network error before sending to /api/report/generate
  App uses bundled English template strings:
    Template: "Your credit score is {score} (Grade {grade}, {risk_level} Risk).
               Your strengths include {pos1} and {pos2}. The main areas to
               improve are {neg1} and {neg2}. Work on reducing your debt burden
               and building savings to improve your score."
  Suggestions use pre-written template per negative factor

FALLBACK SCENARIO 2 — GROQ API TIMEOUT (Server has internet, Groq does not respond):
  Backend waits max 8 seconds for Groq response
  If timeout → backend returns:
    { "status": "fallback", "explanation": "<template text>", "suggestions": [...] }
  App receives this and renders the template version
  User does not see an error — just a slightly simpler explanation

FALLBACK SCENARIO 3 — GROQ RETURNS INVALID JSON:
  Backend catches JSON parse error
  Backend constructs explanation from template using the input payload
  Returns template response with status "fallback"

FALLBACK SCENARIO 4 — VERIFICATION API UNAVAILABLE:
  App retries up to 3 times with 2-second delay
  After 3 failures → show user: "Verification service temporarily unavailable.
  Your progress is saved. Please try again in a few minutes."
  Step marked as PENDING_VERIFICATION
  On retry → resume from same step, no data loss

================================================================================
SECTION 7 — MONGODB COLLECTIONS (SIMULATED DATABASE)
================================================================================

  otp_db:
    { mobile, otp, created_at, expires_at, verified }

  aadhaar_db:
    { aadhaar, name, dob, state, pin, status }

  pan_db:
    { pan, name, dob, pan_active, itr_filed, itr_years }

  ifsc_db:
    { ifsc, bank_name, branch_name, city, state }

  bank_accounts_db:
    { account_number, ifsc, account_holder, account_type, account_active }

  loan_accounts_db:
    { account_number, has_active_loans, loans: [{type, emi_amount, remaining_months}] }

  vehicle_rc_db:
    { vehicle_number, owner_name, vehicle_class, chassis_number,
      engine_number, registration_date, rc_expiry, fitness_expiry }

  eshram_db:
    { uan, name, worker_category, registration_date, status }

  pmsym_db:
    { uan, status, months_contributed, last_contribution_date }

  insurance_db:
    { policy_number, policy_type, policy_holder, insurer,
      sum_insured, premium_annual, policy_start, policy_expiry,
      vehicle_number (for vehicle policies) }

  NO USER DOCUMENTS ARE STORED IN ANY COLLECTION.
  NO BANK TRANSACTION DATA IS STORED.
  NO FEATURE VECTORS OR SHAP DATA IS STORED.
  REPORT LOGS STORE ONLY: hashed_device_id, timestamp, language, status.

================================================================================
SECTION 8 — WHAT BACKEND STORES (LOGGING ONLY)
================================================================================

  The backend maintains a report_logs collection for monitoring only:

  report_logs:
    {
      log_id: <uuid>,
      hashed_device_id: <SHA256 of device fingerprint>,
      request_timestamp: <ISO 8601>,
      language: "Tamil",
      model_used: "llama3-70b-8192",
      response_status: "success" | "fallback" | "error",
      response_time_ms: 820
    }

  NO credit score is stored in logs.
  NO pillar scores are stored in logs.
  NO user name, Aadhaar, PAN, or any identifier is stored in logs.
  Logs are for operational monitoring only (latency, error rates).

================================================================================
SECTION 9 — ERROR CODES REFERENCE
================================================================================

  HTTP 200 — Success
  HTTP 400 — Bad Request (invalid input format)
  HTTP 401 — Unauthorized (HMAC mismatch or invalid API key)
  HTTP 404 — Record not found (identifier not in database)
  HTTP 422 — Validation Error (missing required fields)
  HTTP 429 — Too Many Requests (rate limit exceeded)
  HTTP 500 — Internal Server Error (unexpected failure)
  HTTP 503 — Service Unavailable (Groq API down)

  All error responses follow this format:
  {
    "error": "<error_code>",
    "message": "<human readable description>",
    "timestamp": "<ISO 8601>"
  }

================================================================================
SECTION 10 — PERFORMANCE TARGETS
================================================================================

  Verification API endpoints  : 50–150 ms per call
  /api/report/generate        : 800–2000 ms total
    - Prompt build            : < 10 ms
    - Groq API call           : 700–1800 ms
    - Response parse          : < 10 ms
  OTP send                    : < 200 ms
  OTP verify                  : < 50 ms

  SERVER UPTIME TARGET        : 99.5% (demo/production)
  MAX CONCURRENT REQUESTS     : 100 (single Docker instance)

================================================================================
SECTION 11 — COMPLETE END-TO-END FLOW SUMMARY
================================================================================

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                     GIGCREDIT FULL SYSTEM FLOW                     │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  [USER OPENS APP]
         │
         ▼
  [STEP 1] User enters profile + mobile number
         │ App → POST /auth/otp/send → Server
         │ User enters OTP
         │ App → POST /auth/otp/verify → Server
         │ OTP verified ✓
         ▼
  [STEP 2] User uploads Aadhaar + PAN + Selfie
         │ On-device: OCR extracts fields
         │ App → POST /gov/aadhaar/verify → Server
         │ App → POST /gov/pan/verify → Server
         │ On-device: Name cross-match, face match
         │ Identity verified ✓
         ▼
  [STEP 3] User uploads bank statement
         │ On-device: PDF parsed, transactions extracted (entirely on-device)
         │ App → POST /bank/ifsc/verify → Server
         │ App → POST /bank/account/verify → Server
         │ App → POST /bank/loan/check → Server
         │ On-device: All transaction analysis, EMI detection, feature computation
         │ Bank verified ✓
         ▼
  [STEP 4] User uploads utility bills
         │ On-device: OCR extracts all bill data
         │ On-device: Cross-check bill amounts vs bank debits
         │ NO backend call in Step 4
         │ Utility evidence verified ✓
         ▼
  [STEP 5] Work proof (dynamic per work type)
         │ On-device: OCR extracts work documents
         │ Platform Workers → App → POST /gov/vehicle/rc/verify → Server
         │ Vendors, Tradespeople, Freelancers → NO backend call
         │ Work identity verified ✓
         ▼
  [STEP 6] Government scheme verification
         │ App → POST /gov/eshram/verify → Server
         │ App → POST /gov/pmsym/verify → Server
         │ On-device: Social signals stored in VerifiedProfile
         │ Scheme signals verified ✓
         ▼
  [STEP 7] Insurance verification
         │ App → POST /gov/insurance/policy/verify → Server (1–3 calls)
         │ On-device: Policy cross-check vs Aadhaar name, vehicle number
         │ Insurance verified ✓
         ▼
  [ON-DEVICE SCORING]
         │ Feature engineering → 88 features (7 pillars)
         │ NaN sanitization
         │ P1–P4 → XGBoost m2cgen Dart scorers
         │ P5, P7 → Dart Scorecards
         │ P6 → RandomForest m2cgen Dart scorer
         │ Meta-learner → Logistic Regression dot product
         │ Final score: 300–900
         │ Grade + Risk Band assigned
         ▼
  [ON-DEVICE SHAP LOOKUP]
         │ shap_lookup.json (bundled in app)
         │ 88 features → SHAP impact values
         │ Top 3 positive + Top 3 negative selected
         │ feature_label (human-readable) assigned per feature
         ▼
  [ON-DEVICE PAYLOAD BUILD]
         │ Assemble Explanation JSON (Section 5.2)
         │ No raw data, no identifiers, no transactions
         ▼
  [APP → BACKEND]
         │ POST /api/report/generate
         │ HMAC-signed + API key authenticated
         ▼
  [BACKEND PROMPT BUILD]
         │ Fill prompt template with payload fields
         │ Set language from payload
         ▼
  [BACKEND → GROQ API]
         │ POST to Groq (llama3-70b-8192)
         │ temperature=0.4, max_tokens=600, json_object mode
         ▼
  [GROQ RETURNS RESPONSE]
         │ { "explanation": "...", "suggestions": ["...", "...", "..."] }
         ▼
  [BACKEND VALIDATES + FORMATS]
         │ Parse JSON, validate fields
         │ Return formatted response to app
         ▼
  [BACKEND → APP]
         │ { "status": "success", "explanation": "...", "suggestions": [...] }
         ▼
  [ON-DEVICE — FINAL REPORT ASSEMBLY]
         │ Merge: score + pillars + SHAP + LLM explanation + suggestions
         │ Final Report object assembled
         ▼
  [ON-DEVICE — RENDER + PDF]
         │ Flutter UI renders report screen
         │ User can export as PDF (on-device PDF generation)
         │ Report displayed to user ✓

  END OF FLOW.
  The backend is not involved after returning the LLM response.
  All final report assembly, rendering, and PDF export are on-device.

================================================================================
END OF BACKEND SERVER SPECIFICATION

GIGCREDIT — MASTER PROJECT SPECIFICATION

THIS DOCUMENT COVERS:
  - What GigCredit is and the problem it solves
  - Who the users are
  - Complete 9-step onboarding flow (every input, every field)
  - On-device processing (OCR, validation, feature engineering)
  - Evaluation engine (7 pillars, 95 features)

================================================================================
PART 1 — WHAT IS GIGCREDIT
================================================================================

GigCredit is a privacy-first, on-device credit scoring mobile application
built for Indian gig workers who have no CIBIL credit history.

Millions of gig workers in India — delivery partners, street vendors,
electricians, plumbers, freelancers — are denied credit by traditional
banks because standard underwriting depends on salary slips, CIBIL scores,
and formal employment history. Gig workers have none of these.

GigCredit solves this by converting REAL FINANCIAL BEHAVIOR into a credit
score (300–900). The scoring happens entirely on the user's mobile device.
No sensitive data leaves the device for scoring purposes.

After scoring, GigCredit connects verified gig workers to NBFC and fintech
lenders through an in-app loan application marketplace.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.1 CORE VALUE PROPOSITION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FOR GIG WORKERS:
  - Get a credit score based on real behavior, not salary slips
  - Understand exactly why you got your score (explainability)
  - Apply for loans without visiting a bank
  - Improve your score with personalized suggestions

FOR LENDERS (NBFCs / Fintechs):
  - Access a large underserved borrower segment
  - Receive pre-qualified, decision-ready applicants
  - Behavioral trust signals beyond bureau data
  - Lower customer acquisition cost

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.2 WHO USES GIGCREDIT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FOUR WORK TYPES SUPPORTED:

  1. PLATFORM WORKER
     Examples: Swiggy delivery, Zomato delivery, Ola driver, Uber driver,
               Rapido captain, Porter captain, Dunzo agent, Blinkit partner
     Key identifier: Has a registered gig platform account and vehicle

  2. VENDOR / SELLER
     Examples: Fruit seller, vegetable vendor, street food, kirana shop,
               small goods seller
     Key identifier: May have SVANidhi scheme registration

  3. SKILLED TRADESPERSON
     Examples: Electrician, plumber, carpenter, welder, mason, tailor,
               mechanic, painter
     Key identifier: Has trade licence or skill certificate

  4. FREELANCER / CREATIVE PROFESSIONAL
     Examples: Designer, developer, writer, content creator, consultant,
               photographer
     Key identifier: Receives payments via Payoneer, Wise, Upwork, Fiverr

Work type is selected in Step 1 and determines:
  - Entire content of Step 5 (work proof documents)
  - P1 income feature engineering routing
  - Meta-learner work-type interaction terms
  - Vehicle insurance mandatory status in Step 7

================================================================================

================================================================================
PART 3 — 9-STEP ONBOARDING FLOW
================================================================================

OVERVIEW:
  Total user-facing steps    : 9
  Internal automated step    : 1 (EMI Auto-Analysis, runs after Step 3)
  Steps with dynamic content : Step 5 (changes per work type)
  Steps with conditional     : Step 4 (rent), Step 7 (vehicle insurance)
  Backend API calls          : Steps 1, 2, 3, 5 (platform only), 6, 7, 8

STEP STATE MODEL:
  Each step has one of these states:
    NOT_STARTED
    IN_PROGRESS
    OCR_COMPLETE
    PENDING_VERIFICATION
    VERIFIED
    REJECTED

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 1 — BASIC PROFILE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Collect the user's fundamental personal and professional details.
  Capture work type to configure Step 5 and all downstream feature
  engineering. Verify mobile number via OTP.

BACKEND CALL: /auth/otp/send + /auth/otp/verify

HOW PRESENTED:
  Multi-section scrollable form with text fields, date picker,
  dropdown selectors, and a four-option work type card selector.

SECTION A — PERSONAL DETAILS:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  1  | Full Name              | Single-line text    | Plain text          | Yes
  2  | Date of Birth          | Date picker         | DD/MM/YYYY          | Yes
  3  | Mobile Number          | Numeric field       | 10-digit            | Yes
  4  | OTP Verification       | OTP input (6-digit) | Numeric             | Yes
  5  | Current Address        | Multi-line text     | Free text           | Yes
  6  | Permanent Address      | Multi-line text     | Free text           | Yes
  7  | State of Residence     | Dropdown            | 28 states + 8 UTs   | Yes

SECTION B — PROFESSIONAL DETAILS:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  8  | Work Type              | 4-card selector     | One of 4 types      | Yes
  9  | Self-Declared Income   | Numeric field (₹)   | Integer per month   | Yes
  10 | Years in Profession    | Numeric stepper     | Integer 0–40        | Yes
  11 | Number of Dependents   | Numeric stepper     | Integer 0–10        | Yes
  12 | Vehicle Ownership      | Radio Yes / No      | Boolean             | Yes
  13 | Secondary Income       | Text + amount field | Free text + ₹       | Optional

STEP 1 TOTAL: 13 fields | Mandatory: 12 | Optional: 1

KEY OUTPUTS FROM STEP 1:
  - work_type → drives Step 5 layout + feature routing
  - vehicle_ownership → drives Step 7 vehicle insurance requirement
  - state_of_residence → sets ANCHOR for income normalization
  - mobile_verified = true after OTP

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 2 — IDENTITY VERIFICATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Verify government-issued identity documents and confirm the person
  physically present matches the identity on file.

BACKEND CALLS: /gov/aadhaar/verify + /gov/pan/verify

HOW PRESENTED:
  Three upload cards (Aadhaar, PAN, Selfie) with camera/gallery options.
  Each card shows a guide overlay illustrating correct photo angle.

AADHAAR CARD:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  1  | Aadhaar Number         | Segmented text      | 12-digit numeric    | Yes
  2  | Aadhaar Front Image    | Upload / Camera     | JPG or PNG          | Yes
  3  | Aadhaar Back Image     | Upload / Camera     | JPG or PNG          | Yes

  Why both sides:
    Front → name, DOB, Aadhaar number, photo
    Back  → full address, state, PIN code (for cross-match with utility bills)

PAN CARD:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  4  | PAN Number             | Alphanumeric text   | ABCDE1234F format   | Yes
  5  | PAN Card Photo         | Upload / Camera     | JPG or PNG          | Yes

LIVE SELFIE:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  6  | Live Selfie            | In-app camera only  | Camera capture only | Yes

  Why camera-only: Gallery uploads could be a photo of a photo.
  Camera-only with liveness detection ensures physical presence.
  On-device face matching: selfie vs Aadhaar front photo (MobileFaceNet).

ON-DEVICE PROCESSING AFTER STEP 2:
  - OCR extracts: name, DOB, Aadhaar number, address, state from card images
  - API returns: name, DOB, state from government database
  - Cross-match: API name vs OCR name (fuzzy match ≥ 85%)
  - Face match: selfie vs Aadhaar photo (similarity ≥ 0.70)
  - aadhaar_verified = true if API match + face match pass
  - pan_verified = true if API match passes
  - itr_filed status stored from PAN API for Step 8

STEP 2 TOTAL: 6 fields | Mandatory: 6 | Optional: 0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 3 — BANK VERIFICATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Collect bank transaction history (minimum 6 months) to evaluate income
  patterns, expense behaviour, EMI obligations, savings habits, and debt
  indicators. This is the most data-rich step — produces majority of P1–P4
  features.

BACKEND CALLS: /bank/ifsc/verify + /bank/account/verify + /bank/loan/check

HOW PRESENTED:
  Primary bank section (visible by default), expandable secondary bank
  section, and optional UPI section below both.

PRIMARY BANK ACCOUNT:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  1  | Bank Name              | Searchable dropdown | Indian banks list   | Yes
  2  | Account Holder Name    | Single-line text    | Plain text          | Yes
  3  | Bank Branch Name       | Single-line text    | Plain text          | Yes
  4  | IFSC Code              | Alphanumeric        | 11 characters       | Yes
  5  | Account Number         | Numeric             | 9–18 digits         | Yes
  6  | Bank Statement PDF     | PDF file picker     | Min 6 months        | Yes
  7  | MICR Code              | Numeric             | 9-digit             | Optional

SECONDARY BANK ACCOUNT (expandable toggle):

  #  | Field                  | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|----------
  8  | Enable Secondary       | Optional toggle
  9–15 | Same 7 fields        | Optional

  UI tip: "Platform workers often receive gig income in a separate account.
  Adding it gives a more accurate picture of your total earnings."

UPI STATEMENT (optional):

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  16 | UPI Platform           | Dropdown            | PhonePe/GPay/Paytm  | Optional
  17 | UPI Statement PDF      | PDF file picker     | PDF export from app | Optional

ON-DEVICE PROCESSING AFTER STEP 3:
  - Bank statement PDF parsed entirely on-device
  - Every transaction extracted: date, amount, description, balance, mode
  - Tagged: INCOME_GIG, INCOME_SALARY, EMI_DEBIT, UTILITY_DEBIT, RENT, ATM
  - Monthly aggregates computed: credits, debits, closing balance
  - IFSC API: confirms bank name and branch
  - Account API: confirms account holder name, active status
  - Loan API: returns active loan list with EMI amounts

STEP 3 TOTAL: 17 fields | Mandatory: 6 | Optional: 11

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
INTERNAL STEP — EMI AUTO-ANALYSIS (AUTOMATED, NO USER INPUT)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Automatically detect recurring EMI debits from the parsed bank statement.
  Runs immediately after Step 3 bank statement parsing completes.
  No user interaction required.

WHAT IT DOES:
  - Scans all debit transactions for keywords:
    EMI, LOAN, INSTALLMENT, NACH EMI, ECS EMI, BAJAJ, HDFC LOAN, ICICI EMI
  - Identifies recurring debits with consistent amounts and monthly intervals
  - Tags each detected EMI with: lender_name, emi_amount, detected_date
  - Stores detected_emis list in VerifiedProfile
  - This data is later cross-referenced with user-declared EMIs in Step 9

OUTPUT:
  auto_detected_emis = [
    { lender: "HDFC Bank", emi_amount: 3500, last_debit: "2026-03-05" },
    { lender: "Bajaj Finance", emi_amount: 1800, last_debit: "2026-03-07" }
  ]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 4 — UTILITY BILL VERIFICATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Evaluate payment discipline and financial consistency through 6 months
  of regular household bill payments. Bills cross-referenced against bank
  statement to confirm actual payment.

BACKEND CALL: None (entirely on-device)

HOW PRESENTED:
  Three mandatory accordion panels (Electricity, LPG, Mobile), each with a
  6-slot upload grid labelled by month. Optional panels below.

ELECTRICITY BILLS (Mandatory):

  #  | Field                  | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------
  1–6 | Bills Month 1–6       | JPG, PNG, or PDF    | 6 bills required

  OCR extracts per bill: consumer number, service address, bill month,
  units consumed, amount due, due date, payment status.
  Consumer number must be identical across all 6 bills.

LPG / GAS BILLS (Mandatory):

  #  | Field                  | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------
  7–12 | Bills Month 1–6      | JPG, PNG, or PDF    | 6 bills required

  Accepted providers: Indane, HP Gas, Bharat Gas
  OCR extracts: consumer number, distributor, month, cylinders, amount.

MOBILE PHONE BILLS (Mandatory):

  #  | Field                  | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------
  13–18 | Bills Month 1–6     | JPG, PNG, or PDF    | 6 bills required

  Accepted: Jio, Airtel, Vi, BSNL postpaid bills or prepaid receipts
  OCR extracts: mobile number, provider, month, plan type, amount.
  Mobile number must match Step 1 mobile number.

RENT PROOF (Conditional — shown only if user is a renter):

  User selects ONE of three options:
  #  | Option                 | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------
  19 | Rental Agreement       | PDF or image        | Conditional
  20 | Monthly Rent Receipts  | 6-slot upload grid  | Conditional
  21 | Bank Auto-Detection    | Automatic (no upload)| Conditional

  Option C pre-selected if bank parser found RENT / HOUSE RENT in debits.

OPTIONAL UTILITIES:

  22 | WiFi / Broadband Bills | Up to 6 bills       | Optional
  23 | OTT Subscription       | Screenshots / PDF   | Optional

ON-DEVICE PROCESSING AFTER STEP 4:
  - OCR extracts consumer number, month, amount, due date from each bill
  - Cross-checks: bill debit amount found in bank statement
  - On-time flag: payment date ≤ due date
  - Consumer/mobile number consistency across 6 months verified
  - All checks done entirely on-device, no backend call

STEP 4 TOTAL: 18 mandatory + up to 13 optional

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Verify occupation, work history, and work-specific income sources.
  Entirely reconfigured based on work type from Step 1.

BACKEND CALL: /gov/vehicle/rc/verify (Platform Workers only)

  #  | Field                  | Format              | Mandatory

  4  | Driving Licence Back   | JPG or PNG          | Yes
  5  | Vehicle Insurance Cert | JPG, PNG, or PDF    | Yes

SECTION 2: PLATFORM EARNING SCREENSHOTS (3 months)

  #  | Field                  | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------
  6  | Screenshot Month 1     | JPG or PNG          | Yes
  7  | Screenshot Month 2     | JPG or PNG          | Yes
  8  | Screenshot Month 3     | JPG or PNG          | Yes
  9  | Second Platform App    | JPG or PNG          | Optional
  10 | Third Platform App     | JPG or PNG          | Optional

  OCR extracts per screenshot: monthly earnings total, number of trips,
  platform rating, account name, joined date.
  API verifies: RC validity, DL validity, DL class vs RC class match.

STEP 5A TOTAL: 10 fields | Mandatory: 8 | Optional: 2

  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------

  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------

  Policy holder name must match Aadhaar name.
