--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 8 — TAX AND COMPLIANCE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Collect ITR (Income Tax Return) and GST filing records as compliance
  and income declaration indicators.
  Feeds P3 (Debt Management) and P6 (Financial Resilience) features.

BACKEND CALL: /gov/income-tax/itr/verify

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  1  | ITR Acknowledgement    | Upload / Camera     | PDF or JPG          | Optional
  2  | Assessment Year        | Dropdown            | e.g. 2024–25        | If submitting
  3  | ITR Form Type          | Dropdown            | ITR-1/2/3/4         | If submitting
  4  | GST Registration No    | Text field          | 15-char alphanumeric| Optional
  5  | GST Returns (6 months) | Multi-file upload   | PDF per return      | If GST reg

  API verifies: ITR filing status, assessment year, gross vs net income.
  All fields optional — filing ITR and GST increases score confidence.

STEP 8 TOTAL: 5 fields | Mandatory: 0 | Optional: 5

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 9 — EMI AND LOAN BEHAVIOUR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PURPOSE:
  Capture the user's existing loan and EMI obligations.
  Feeds P3 (Debt Management) scoring pillar.
  Cross-referenced against auto-detected EMIs from Step 3.

BACKEND CALL: None (cross-check is on-device vs Step 3 bank data)

TOP-LEVEL TOGGLE:

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|----------
  1  | Active Loans / EMIs?   | Toggle Yes / No     | Required

  If No → Step 9 marked complete. No further inputs needed.
  If Yes → Show loan entry cards (up to 5 entries).

LOAN ENTRY CARD (Repeatable, up to 5):

  #  | Field                  | UI Element          | Format              | Mandatory
  ---|------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|----------
  2  | Lender Name            | Dropdown + free text| Bank/NBFC/MFI/Other | Required per entry
  3  | Monthly EMI Amount (₹) | Numeric field       | Positive integer    | Required per entry
  4  | Previous Debit Date    | Date picker         | DD/MM/YYYY          | Required per entry
  5  | Latest Debit Date      | Date picker         | DD/MM/YYYY          | Required per entry
  6  | Loan Verification      | Action button       | Triggers on-device  | Optional per entry

  Loan Verification button: cross-checks declared EMI against auto-detected
  EMIs from Step 3 bank statement. Shows: Matched / Not Found tag.

MULTI-ENTRY RULES:
  - Minimum entries (if Yes): 1
  - Maximum entries: 5
  - "Add Another Loan" button after each card
  - "Remove" icon on each card
  - All required fields must be filled before Continue

STEP 9 TOTAL:
  1 toggle + (4 required × up to 5 entries) + (1 optional × up to 5 entries)
  Maximum: 26 fields | Minimum: 6 fields (1 entry + toggle)

================================================================================
PART 4 — ON-DEVICE VALIDATION SYSTEM
================================================================================

During Steps 1–9, three levels of validation run entirely on-device:

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.1 INDIVIDUAL FIELD VALIDATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Runs immediately when user fills each field.
  Examples:
  - Aadhaar: must be exactly 12 numeric digits
  - PAN: must match [A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1} regex
  - IFSC: must match [A-Z]{4}0[A-Z0-9]{6} regex
  - Mobile: must be 10 digits, starts with 6–9
  - Dates: previous date must be before latest date
  - EMI amount: must be positive integer > 0
  - Bank statement: must be PDF, must cover minimum 6 months

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.2 CROSS-INTERNAL VALIDATION (Within Same Step)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Runs when user submits a step.
  Examples:
  - Step 2: Aadhaar OCR name vs PAN OCR name must match (fuzzy ≥ 85%)
  - Step 2: Selfie face vs Aadhaar photo face similarity ≥ 0.70
  - Step 4: Consumer number identical across all 6 electricity bills
  - Step 4: Mobile number on bills matches Step 1 mobile number
  - Step 5A: DL name must match Aadhaar name
  - Step 5A: DL vehicle class must match RC vehicle class
  - Step 9: Latest debit date must be ≥ Previous debit date

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4.3 GLOBAL CROSS-STEP VALIDATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Runs after all steps are complete (before feature engineering begins).
  Examples:
  - Step 5A RC vehicle number must match Step 7 vehicle insurance policy
  - Step 3 bank account holder name must match Step 2 Aadhaar name
  - Step 3 detected EMIs cross-referenced with Step 9 declared EMIs
  - Step 4 utility bill address must match Step 2 Aadhaar back address
  - Step 8 ITR declared income vs Step 3 average monthly credit × 12

================================================================================
PART 5 — FEATURE ENGINEERING (ON-DEVICE)
================================================================================

After all 9 steps are complete and validation passes:

  - All verified data assembled into VerifiedProfile object
  - Feature engineering runs entirely in Dart on the user's device
  - 95 features computed across 7 pillars
  - All features normalized to Float32 in range [0.0, 1.0]
    (0.0 = worst signal, 1.0 = best signal)
  - NaN / Infinity → replaced with FALLBACK value (default 0.40)
    unless pillar-specific fallback is defined
  - ANCHOR: state median monthly income from Aadhaar state
    (fallback ₹15,000/month national median if state not found)

FEATURE DISTRIBUTION ACROSS 7 PILLARS:

  Pillar  | Name                   | Feature Indices | Count
  --------|------------------------|-----------------|-------
  P1      | Income Stability       | 0–12            | 13
  P2      | Payment Discipline     | 13–27           | 15
  P3      | Debt Management        | 28–36           | 9
  P4      | Savings Behaviour      | 37–48           | 12
  P5      | Work and Identity      | 49–66           | 18
  P6      | Financial Resilience   | 67–77           | 11
  P7      | Social Accountability  | 78–94           | 17
  --------|------------------------|-----------------|-------
  TOTAL   |                        |                 | 95

HARD SCORING BLOCKS (evaluated before any feature computation):
  - aadhaar_verified = false → BLOCK (identity not verified)
  - face_similarity_score < 0.70 → BLOCK (face match failed)
  - bank_statement_absent = true → BLOCK (bank statement required)
  If any block triggers → scoring does not proceed.
  User shown: "Please complete required verification to proceed."

================================================================================
PART 6 — EVALUATION ENGINE (ON-DEVICE)
================================================================================

The evaluation engine runs entirely on the user's Flutter device.
Models are exported from Python to pure Dart code via m2cgen.
Zero runtime ML dependencies — pure Dart arithmetic.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6.1 7 PILLAR MODELS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Pillar | Name                   | Model Type              | Dart File
  -------|------------------------|-------------------------|------------------
  P1     | Income Stability       | XGBoost (m2cgen Dart)   | p1_scorer.dart
  P2     | Payment Discipline     | XGBoost (m2cgen Dart)   | p2_scorer.dart
  P3     | Debt Management        | XGBoost (m2cgen Dart)   | p3_scorer.dart
  P4     | Savings Behaviour      | XGBoost (m2cgen Dart)   | p4_scorer.dart
  P5     | Work and Identity      | Dart Scorecard          | scorecard_p5.dart
  P6     | Financial Resilience   | RandomForest (m2cgen)   | p6_scorer.dart
  P7     | Social Accountability  | Dart Scorecard          | scorecard_p7.dart

  P1–P4, P6: ML models trained offline in Python, exported to Dart via m2cgen
  P5, P7: Deterministic weighted sum scorecards, hand-written in Dart
  All pillar outputs: float in [0.0, 1.0]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6.2 META-LEARNER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Type   : Logistic Regression (pure Dart dot product + sigmoid)
  File   : scoring_constants.dart (coefficients embedded as constants)

  Input  : 19 values total
    - 7 pillar scores (confidence-adjusted)
    - 4 work-type one-hot flags (platform / vendor / trades / freelancer)
    - 8 interaction terms (P1 × work-type, P2 × work-type for each type)

  Formula:
    logit = dot(meta_inputs, coefficients) + intercept
    probability = 1 / (1 + exp(-logit))
    final_score = round(probability × 600) + 300

  Output range: 300 (worst) → 900 (best)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6.3 SCORE GRADES AND RISK BANDS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Score     | Grade | Label           | Risk Band
  ----------|-------|-----------------|----------
  800–900   | S     | Exceptional     | Low
  720–799   | A     | Excellent       | Low
  640–719   | B     | Good            | Low
  560–639   | C     | Average         | Medium
  480–559   | D     | Below Average   | Medium
  300–479   | E     | Poor            | High

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6.4 HARD RULES (Applied After Model Inference)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  DEBT BAND CAP:
    If emi_to_income_ratio > 0.80:
      P3 score capped at 0.30 regardless of model output
      Applied AFTER model inference, BEFORE confidence adjustment

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6.5 EXECUTION ORDER (Sequential, No Isolates Needed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  1.  features = engineerFeatures(verifiedProfile)   // 95 features
  2.  features = sanitizeFeatures(features)           // NaN guard
  3.  p1Raw = scoreP1(features[0..12])               // XGBoost Dart
  4.  p2Raw = scoreP2(features[13..27])              // XGBoost Dart
  5.  p3Raw = scoreP3(features[28..36])              // XGBoost Dart
  6.  p4Raw = scoreP4(features[37..48])              // XGBoost Dart
  7.  p5Raw = scorecardP5(features[49..66])          // Dart scorecard
  8.  p6Raw = scoreP6(features[67..77])              // RandomForest Dart
  9.  p7Raw = scorecardP7(features[78..94])          // Dart scorecard
  10. apply debt band cap to p3Raw if needed
  11. validate all pillar outputs (clamp, NaN check)
  12. apply confidence adjustment to each pillar
  13. finalScore = metaLearner(adjustedPillars, workType)
  14. grade = assignGrade(finalScore)
  15. riskBand = assignRiskBand(finalScore)

  Total inference time: 15–20ms on mid-range Android device

================================================================================
PART 7 — CONFIDENCE ENGINE (ON-DEVICE)
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
  Each user input across Steps 1–9 has a data quality level.
  The Confidence Engine processes each input's confidence and computes
  a per-pillar confidence score that adjusts the pillar's raw ML score.

HOW CONFIDENCE IS ASSIGNED PER INPUT:

  Data Quality Level        | Confidence Value | Example
  --------------------------|------------------|----------------------------
  API verified              | 1.00             | Aadhaar API match confirmed
  OCR extracted + verified  | 0.90             | Bill amount matches bank
  OCR extracted only        | 0.75             | Bill month matches pattern
  User declared + bank match| 0.85             | EMI matches bank debit
  User declared only        | 0.60             | Self-declared income
  Optional doc not provided | 0.40             | No insurance submitted
  Missing / fallback used   | 0.20             | Field absent, default used

PER-PILLAR CONFIDENCE SCORE:
  Computed as weighted average of confidence values of all features
  that feed that pillar.

CONFIDENCE ADJUSTMENT FORMULA:
  adjusted_score = raw_score × confidence + 0.50 × (1 − confidence)

  Effect:
    confidence = 1.00 → adjusted = raw_score (no change)
    confidence = 0.50 → adjusted = 0.50 (neutral, no strong signal)
    confidence = 0.00 → adjusted = 0.50 (forced neutral)

MINIMUM CONFIDENCE THRESHOLD:
  If pillar confidence < 0.30:
    → Pillar excluded from meta-learner
    → Set to 0.50 neutral
    → Report shows "Not enough data for this pillar"

MINIMUM SCORING GATE (must pass before final score is shown):
  - Step 1 complete: required
  - Step 2 identity verified: required
  - Step 3 bank statement parsed with ≥ 30 transactions: required
  If gate not satisfied → show "Complete required steps to see your score"

================================================================================
PART 8 — SHAP EXPLAINABILITY (ON-DEVICE)
================================================================================

PURPOSE:
  Explain to the user WHY they got their score by identifying which
  specific features helped or hurt their score the most.

HOW IT WORKS:
  - shap_lookup.json is pre-computed offline during ML pipeline training
  - Bundled inside the app at install time (size: ~8–12 KB)
  - For each of 95 features, the JSON stores binned SHAP impact values

SHAP LOOKUP FORMAT:
  {
    "feature_name": {
      "bins": [0.0, 0.1, 0.2, ..., 1.0],
      "shap_values": [impact_bin_0, impact_bin_1, ..., impact_bin_9]
    }
  }

ON-DEVICE LOOKUP PROCESS:
  1. For each feature, find which bin the feature value falls into
  2. Read the pre-computed SHAP impact for that bin
  3. Collect all (feature, impact) pairs across all 95 features
  4. Sort by absolute impact descending
  5. Select top 3 POSITIVE impacts (strengths)
  6. Select top 3 NEGATIVE impacts (areas to improve)
  7. Map internal feature names to human-readable labels

  Example mapping:
    "emi_to_income_ratio"     → "EMI payments high relative to income"
    "income_stability_cv"     → "Consistent monthly income"
    "utility_on_time_ratio"   → "Utility bills paid on time"
    "health_insurance_active" → "No active health insurance"

SHAP IS FOR EXPLANATION ONLY:
  SHAP values do NOT affect the credit score.
  The score is computed solely by the meta-learner.

WHAT GETS SENT TO BACKEND (LLM):
  Only the SHAP output (top 3 positive + top 3 negative with labels).
  The evaluation engine score and pillar breakdown go DIRECTLY to
  the report template — they are NOT sent through the LLM.

================================================================================
PART 9 — BACKEND SERVER PROCESSING
================================================================================

The backend server has exactly TWO responsibilities:

  1. VERIFICATION API — during onboarding Steps 1–9
  2. LLM REPORT GENERATION — after on-device scoring completes

The backend NEVER:
  - Computes credit scores
  - Runs ML models
  - Processes bank statements
  - Stores Aadhaar, PAN, bank data, feature vectors, or scores

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.1 VERIFICATION API LAYER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  During onboarding, the app sends ONLY minimal identifiers to the server.
  The server queries MongoDB (simulated government/financial database).
  The server returns matching record fields.
  The app does all cross-validation on-device after receiving the response.

  VERIFICATION CALLS BY STEP:

  Step 1 : /auth/otp/send + /auth/otp/verify
  Step 2 : /gov/aadhaar/verify + /gov/pan/verify
  Step 3 : /bank/ifsc/verify + /bank/account/verify + /bank/loan/check
  Step 4 : No backend call
  Step 5 : /gov/vehicle/rc/verify (Platform Workers only)
  Step 6 : /gov/eshram/verify + /gov/pmsym/verify
  Step 7 : /gov/insurance/policy/verify (1–3 calls)
  Step 8 : /gov/income-tax/itr/verify
  Step 9 : No backend call (cross-check is on-device vs Step 3 data)

  Total maximum backend calls across all 9 steps: 13 calls
  All calls: lightweight (identifier only), response: 50–150ms

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.2 LLM REPORT GENERATION LAYER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WHAT THE APP SENDS (Explanation Payload):

  {
    "credit_score": 682,
    "grade": "B",
    "risk_level": "Medium",
    "work_type": "platform_worker",
    "language": "Tamil",
    "pillar_scores": {
      "income_stability": 72,
      "payment_discipline": 68,
      "debt_management": 55,
      "savings_behaviour": 61,
      "work_identity": 78,
      "financial_resilience": 45,
      "social_accountability": 60
    },
    "positive_factors": [
      { "label": "Consistent monthly income", "pillar": "Income Stability", "impact": 15 },
      { "label": "Utility bills paid on time", "pillar": "Payment Discipline", "impact": 12 },
      { "label": "Bank balance growing steadily", "pillar": "Savings Behaviour", "impact": 9 }
    ],
    "negative_factors": [
      { "label": "EMI payments high relative to income", "pillar": "Debt Management", "impact": -18 },
      { "label": "No active health insurance", "pillar": "Financial Resilience", "impact": -10 },
      { "label": "Low monthly savings rate", "pillar": "Savings Behaviour", "impact": -7 }
    ],
    "confidence_level": "High"
  }

  NOTE: No Aadhaar, PAN, bank transactions, or raw feature values sent.
  NOTE: Credit score and pillar scores are in the payload for context
        but the LLM does NOT modify them. They go directly to report template.

WHAT THE BACKEND DOES:
  1. Receive payload (HMAC authenticated)
  2. Build structured Groq prompt from payload fields
  3. Call Groq API (model: llama3-70b-8192, temperature: 0.4)
  4. Parse LLM response JSON
  5. Return formatted response to app

WHAT GROQ RETURNS:
  {
    "explanation": "4–5 sentence plain language explanation of the score",
    "suggestions": [
      "Actionable suggestion 1 based on top negative factor",
      "Actionable suggestion 2 based on second negative factor",
      "Actionable suggestion 3 based on third negative factor"
    ]
  }

  Supported languages: English, Hindi, Tamil, Telugu, Kannada
  Response time: 800–2000ms

WHAT HAPPENS ON-DEVICE AFTER LLM RESPONSE:
  - App validates response (non-empty explanation, exactly 3 suggestions)
  - LLM output merged with on-device score data
  - Full report assembled on-device
  - If LLM unavailable → fallback template text used

================================================================================
PART 10 — REPORT ASSEMBLY (ON-DEVICE)
================================================================================

After LLM response is received, the report is assembled entirely on-device.
The backend is NOT involved in report assembly or PDF generation.

REPORT TEMPLATE — 4 CORE COMPONENTS:

  COMPONENT 1 — SCORE OUTPUT (from Evaluation Engine, direct)
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    - Credit score badge (300–900)
    - Grade (S / A / B / C / D / E)
    - Risk band (Low / Medium / High)
    - 7 pillar score breakdown bars (visual progress bars)
    - Confidence level indicator

  COMPONENT 2 — SHAP EXPLAINABILITY (from SHAP Lookup, direct)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    - Top 3 strengths with human-readable labels and impact values
    - Top 3 concerns with human-readable labels and impact values
    - Visual positive/negative indicator per factor

  COMPONENT 3 — LLM PLAIN ENGLISH EXPLANATION (from Groq via Backend)
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    - 4–5 sentence narrative explaining the score
    - Written in user's selected language
    - References the user's actual strengths and concerns

  COMPONENT 4 — PERSONALIZATION SUGGESTIONS (from Groq via Backend)
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    - 3 specific, actionable improvement tips
    - Based on the top 3 negative SHAP factors
    - Written in user's selected language

ADDITIONAL REPORT SECTIONS:
    - Loan recommendations (from Matching Engine)
    - Score validity indicator (date of assessment)
    - PDF export button (on-device PDF generation)

DATA FLOW INTO REPORT TEMPLATE:

  Evaluation Engine ──► Component 1 (Score + Pillar Breakdown)
  SHAP Lookup       ──► Component 2 (Top Factors)
  Groq LLM          ──► Component 3 (Explanation) + Component 4 (Suggestions)
  Matching Engine   ──► Loan Recommendations Section

================================================================================
PART 11 — DATA DELETION POLICY
================================================================================

WHEN: Immediately after the final report is assembled and displayed.

WHAT IS DELETED:
  - All document images (Aadhaar, PAN, utility bills, RC, insurance)
  - All OCR extracted text
  - Bank statement transaction data
  - Feature vector (95 features)
  - Intermediate SHAP values
  - Pillar raw scores (before confidence adjustment)
  - VerifiedProfile intermediate object

WHAT IS RETAINED (Encrypted Local Storage):
  - Final ScoreReport JSON (score, grade, risk, pillar scores, top factors)
  - LLM explanation text and suggestions
  - Session metadata (completion timestamps)

WHY:
  GigCredit is privacy-first. The raw financial data was only needed
  to generate the score. Once the score and report are generated,
  retaining raw transaction data or document images serves no purpose
  and creates a privacy liability.

BACKEND DATA POLICY:
  Backend never stores any of the above data.
  Only operational logs retained: hashed device ID, timestamp, language,
  response status, response time. No user-identifiable content in logs.

================================================================================
PART 12 — LOAN MATCHING ENGINE AND IN-APP APPLICATION
================================================================================

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12.1 LOAN MATCHING ENGINE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

After report is generated, the matching engine runs on-device:

  USER PROFILE (from report):         PARTNER RULES (from backend config):
  - Credit score: 682                 - Min score requirement
  - Risk band: Medium                 - Risk appetite (Low/Medium/High)
  - Work type: Platform Worker        - Loan slab (min/max amount)
  - Income range: ₹18,000–22,000/mo  - Work type acceptance
  - State: Tamil Nadu                 - State / serviceability

  ENGINE OUTPUT: Eligible lender offers displayed as cards
  Example:
    FinTech A — Eligible up to ₹1,00,000  [Apply Now]
    NBFC B    — Eligible up to ₹50,000    [Apply Now]
    FinTech C — Eligible up to ₹75,000    [Apply Now]

  Offers are INDICATIVE estimates, not guaranteed approvals.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12.2 IN-APP LOAN APPLICATION FLOW
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  [USER TAPS APPLY NOW on a lender card]
         │
         ▼
  [LOAN APPLICATION FORM OPENS INSIDE GIGCREDIT APP]
  (No redirect to external app or browser)
         │
         ▼
  [FORM AUTO-FILLED WITH]:
    - Name, mobile, occupation, income range (from Step 1)
    - GigCredit score, risk level, grade (from report)
    - Requested lender name and offer category
    - GigCredit report attached (summary version)
    - Pillar breakdown summary
         │
         ▼
  [USER ADDS MINIMAL REMAINING FIELDS]:
    - Loan purpose (Dropdown: business, personal, emergency, etc.)
    - Requested loan amount (within eligible range)
    - Address confirmation
    - Any additional lender-required fields
         │
         ▼
  [CONSENT SCREEN]:
    ☑ I allow GigCredit to securely share my application
      with the selected lender.
    [SUBMIT APPLICATION] button
         │
         ▼
  [GIGCREDIT SENDS STRUCTURED APPLICATION TO LENDER API]
    Transferred data:
    - Applicant basic details (name, mobile, occupation)
    - GigCredit score + risk category
    - Behavioral summary (pillar breakdown)
    - KYC verification status (verified / not verified)
    - Requested loan amount
    - Report certificate (structured JSON)
    NOT transferred: raw bank data, Aadhaar, PAN, transaction history
         │
         ▼
  [LENDER REVIEWS APPLICATION]:
    - Eligibility check
    - Internal risk rules
    - KYC validation
    - Fraud screening
    - Rule engine approval
         │
         ▼
  [LENDER DECISION]:
    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ APPROVED    REJECTED    MODIFIED        │
    │ ↓           ↓           ↓               │
    │ User        User        New amount      │
    │ notified    notified    offered         │
    │ in app      in app      to user         │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────┘
         │
         ▼
  [IF APPROVED]:
    - User notified in GigCredit app
    - Lender contacts user directly for agreement
    - Loan disbursed
    - GigCredit earns commission

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12.3 WHY IN-APP (NOT REDIRECT)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  OLD MODEL (Redirect):           NEW MODEL (In-App):
  - User leaves GigCredit         - User stays in GigCredit
  - High drop-off rate            - Higher completion rate
  - No tracking after redirect    - Full journey tracked
  - Weak lender trust signals     - Structured application sent
  - Lower conversion              - Higher conversion
  - No commission visibility      - Clear commission tracking

================================================================================
PART 13 — BUSINESS MODEL
================================================================================

B2C — GIG WORKER (User Revenue):
  - First 3 reports: FREE
  - Subsequent reports: ₹79–₹99 per report
  - Future premium plan: unlimited reports, credit monitoring alerts,
    faster lender offers, priority support

B2B — LENDER PARTNERS (Partner Revenue):
  - Commission per approved and disbursed loan
  - API / SDK integration fee for lender connectivity
  - Premium priority listing in offer cards (future)

ONE-LINE BUSINESS SUMMARY:
  GigCredit converts gig workers into pre-qualified borrowers and enables
  in-app loan applications to multiple lenders, earning revenue from both
  report purchases and successful loan disbursals.

================================================================================
PART 14 — FULL SYSTEM FLOW (END TO END)
================================================================================

  [USER OPENS APP]
    ↓ Login / Register
    ↓ Dashboard → Get Started
    ↓ Input Guidelines (optional) or Continue
    ↓
  [STEP 1] Basic Profile + OTP verification
    ↓ Backend: /auth/otp/send + /auth/otp/verify
    ↓
  [STEP 2] Aadhaar + PAN + Selfie
    ↓ On-device: OCR + face match
    ↓ Backend: /gov/aadhaar/verify + /gov/pan/verify
    ↓ On-device: name cross-match, identity_verified = true
    ↓
  [STEP 3] Bank statement + IFSC + Account
    ↓ On-device: PDF parsed, all transactions extracted and tagged
    ↓ Backend: /bank/ifsc/verify + /bank/account/verify + /bank/loan/check
    ↓ On-device: monthly aggregates, EMI detection, feature signals stored
    ↓
  [INTERNAL] EMI Auto-Analysis (automated, no user input)
    ↓ On-device: recurring debit pattern detection from bank statement
    ↓
  [STEP 4] Utility bills (electricity + LPG + mobile, 6 months each)
    ↓ On-device: OCR, cross-check vs bank debits, on-time flag computed
    ↓ No backend call
    ↓
  [STEP 5] Work proof (dynamic per work type)
    ↓ Platform Workers: RC + DL + platform screenshots
    ↓ Backend (Platform only): /gov/vehicle/rc/verify
    ↓ Vendors / Tradespeople / Freelancers: relevant docs, no backend call
    ↓
  [STEP 6] Government scheme signals
    ↓ Backend: /gov/eshram/verify + /gov/pmsym/verify
    ↓ On-device: scheme participation stored for P7 features
    ↓
  [STEP 7] Insurance policies
    ↓ Backend: /gov/insurance/policy/verify (1–3 calls)
    ↓ On-device: policy cross-check vs Aadhaar name, vehicle number
    ↓
  [STEP 8] Tax and compliance (ITR / GST)
    ↓ Backend: /gov/income-tax/itr/verify
    ↓ On-device: filing status stored for P3 / P6 features
    ↓
  [STEP 9] EMI and loan behaviour (user declares active loans)
    ↓ On-device: cross-check declared EMIs vs auto-detected EMIs (Step 3)
    ↓ No backend call
    ↓
  [GLOBAL CROSS-STEP VALIDATION]
    ↓ On-device: RC vs insurance, Aadhaar address vs utility address, etc.
    ↓
  [FEATURE ENGINEERING]
    ↓ On-device: 95 features computed, normalized [0.0–1.0], NaN sanitized
    ↓
  [EVALUATION ENGINE]
    ↓ On-device: 7 pillar models run (XGBoost × 4, RF × 1, Scorecards × 2)
    ↓ On-device: Meta-learner → Final score 300–900
    ↓
  [CONFIDENCE ENGINE]
    ↓ On-device: Per-pillar confidence computed from input data quality
    ↓ On-device: Pillar scores adjusted by confidence
    ↓
  [SHAP LOOKUP]
    ↓ On-device: shap_lookup.json → 95 features → SHAP impact values
    ↓ On-device: Top 3 positive + Top 3 negative selected, labels assigned
    ↓
  [EXPLANATION PAYLOAD BUILD]
    ↓ On-device: Structured JSON assembled (SHAP factors + score context)
    ↓ No raw data, no identifiers
    ↓
  [APP → BACKEND → GROQ]
    ↓ POST /api/report/generate (HMAC authenticated)
    ↓ Backend builds prompt → Groq (llama3-70b-8192) → plain English text
    ↓ Groq returns: 4–5 sentence explanation + 3 suggestions (in user language)
    ↓ Backend returns response to app
    ↓
  [REPORT ASSEMBLY — ON-DEVICE]
    ↓ Component 1: Score + Grade + Risk + Pillar bars (from Evaluation Engine)
    ↓ Component 2: SHAP top factors (from SHAP Lookup)
    ↓ Component 3: Plain English explanation (from Groq via Backend)
    ↓ Component 4: Personalization suggestions (from Groq via Backend)
    ↓ Loan recommendations (from Matching Engine)
    ↓
  [DATA DELETION]
    ↓ On-device: All raw data deleted (documents, transactions, features)
    ↓ Only ScoreReport JSON retained (encrypted)
    ↓
  [REPORT DISPLAYED TO USER]
    ↓ Score badge, grade, risk band, pillar breakdown
    ↓ Strengths and concerns (SHAP)
    ↓ LLM explanation + suggestions
    ↓ Loan offer cards
    ↓ PDF export option
    ↓
  [USER APPLIES FOR LOAN (OPTIONAL)]
    ↓ Taps Apply Now on a lender card
    ↓ In-app loan form opens (auto-filled)
    ↓ User adds minimal inputs + consent
    ↓ GigCredit sends structured application to lender API
    ↓ Lender reviews → Approve / Reject / Modify
    ↓ User notified in app
    ↓ Loan disbursed → GigCredit earns commission

  END OF FULL SYSTEM FLOW.

================================================================================
PART 15 — ML PIPELINE SUMMARY (OFFLINE — PYTHON)
================================================================================

The ML pipeline runs ONCE offline on a development machine.
Output: trained model code (.dart files) + JSON assets bundled into app.

  STEP 1  : Generate 15,000 synthetic gig worker profiles (95 features each)
  STEP 2  : 80/20 stratified train/validation split (by work type + credit tier)
  STEP 3  : Optuna hyperparameter search (100 trials, 5-fold CV per pillar)
  STEP 4  : Train 4 XGBoost + 1 RandomForest models with best hyperparameters
  STEP 5  : Validate models on held-out set (RMSE targets per pillar)
  STEP 6  : Extract SHAP binned lookup tables → shap_lookup.json
  STEP 7  : Generate cross-validated pillar predictions for meta-learner
  STEP 8  : Train Logistic Regression meta-learner with work-type interactions
  STEP 9  : Export all models to pure Dart via m2cgen
  STEP 10 : Validation gate (Python vs Dart output must match within 1e-5)
  STEP 11 : Copy .dart files + JSON assets to Flutter project

  TOTAL PIPELINE TIME: 30–60 minutes on a modern laptop
  OUTPUT ARTIFACTS:
    p1_scorer.dart, p2_scorer.dart, p3_scorer.dart, p4_scorer.dart
    p6_scorer.dart
    scorecard_p5.dart, scorecard_p7.dart
    scoring_constants.dart (meta-learner coefficients)
    shap_lookup.json (SHAP bins, ~8–12KB)
    meta_coefficients.json (LR weights, ~1KB)
    feature_means.json (fallback values, ~1KB)

================================================================================
END OF GIGCREDIT MASTER PROJECT SPECIFICATION
VERSION 1.0 — COMPLETE PROJECT IDEA AND FLOW
================================================================================

--- specification folders_new\GIGCREDIT-OCR-ENGINE-PARSER-INSTRUCTIONS.txt ---

================================================================================
GIGCREDIT — OCR ENGINE AND PARSER CREATION INSTRUCTION FILE
FOR AI-ASSISTED DEVELOPMENT (VS CODE COPILOT / CURSOR / WINDSURF)
VERSION 1.0
================================================================================

READ THIS FIRST — HOW TO USE THIS FILE
=======================================

This file is an instruction set for an AI coding assistant.

The AI should:
  1. Read this file completely before writing any code
  2. Read all sample input files in the /inputs/ folder (step-wise)
  3. Understand each document type, its layout, and what fields to extract
  4. Generate parsers based on the samples provided
  5. Follow the architecture and output schema defined in this file exactly

Folder structure expected:
  /gigcredit-ocr/
    ├── instructions.txt         ← THIS FILE
    ├── inputs/
    │   ├── step-1/              ← No OCR (user-typed fields only)
    │   ├── step-2/

================================================================================
SECTION 1 — OCR ENGINE ARCHITECTURE
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  All OCR runs on-device inside the Flutter Android app.
  You are generating Python-side parser logic for TESTING and VALIDATION
  of the OCR output. The same logic will be ported to Dart for on-device use.

  For Python testing pipeline:
    - Use pytesseract or paddleocr for OCR extraction
    - Use pdfplumber for text-based PDF extraction (preferred over OCR for PDFs)
    - Use pdf2image for scanned PDF conversion to images
    - Use Pillow for image preprocessing
    - Use re (regex) for field extraction
    - Use fuzzywuzzy or rapidfuzz for name matching

  For Flutter/Dart production:
    - OCR: PaddleOCR Lite via platform channel (Android native)
    - PDF text extraction: syncfusion_flutter_pdf or pdfx
    - Image preprocessing: native Android Bitmap operations
    - Logic: mirror the Python parser logic exactly in Dart

REQUIRED PYTHON LIBRARIES:
  pip install paddleocr pytesseract pdfplumber pdf2image Pillow
  pip install rapidfuzz python-dateutil re2

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.1 GENERAL OCR PIPELINE (APPLIES TO ALL INPUTS)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  STEP 1 — RECEIVE FILE
    Input: file path (jpg / png / pdf)
    Action: detect file type

  STEP 2 — FILE TYPE ROUTING
    if extension in ['.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png', '.webp']:
        route → IMAGE PIPELINE
    if extension == '.pdf':
        try pdfplumber text extraction first
        if text extracted and len > 100 chars:
            route → PDF TEXT PIPELINE (no OCR needed)
        else:
            route → PDF IMAGE PIPELINE (scanned, needs OCR)

  STEP 3 — IMAGE PREPROCESSING (for image pipeline and PDF image pipeline)
    a. Convert to grayscale
    b. Resize: max width 1600px (preserve aspect ratio)
    c. Deskew: correct rotation if > 2 degrees
    d. Denoise: apply mild Gaussian blur
    e. Contrast enhancement: CLAHE (Contrast Limited Adaptive Histogram Eq.)
    f. Binarize: Otsu threshold for black/white conversion

  STEP 4 — OCR EXTRACTION
    Run PaddleOCR on preprocessed image
    Returns: list of (bounding_box, text, confidence)
    Filter: discard any result with confidence < 0.60

  STEP 5 — DOCUMENT TYPE DETECTION
    From raw OCR text, detect document type (see Section 2)
    Select correct parser strategy

  STEP 6 — FIELD EXTRACTION
    Run the selected parser on the OCR text
    Extract target fields into a dict

  STEP 7 — NORMALIZE OUTPUT
    Convert extracted dict to the normalized schema for that document type
    (defined in Section 5)

  STEP 8 — CONFIDENCE SCORING
    Compute parser confidence:
      required_fields_found / total_required_fields
    If confidence < 0.70: flag as LOW_CONFIDENCE, request re-upload

  STEP 9 — CLEANUP
    Delete preprocessed temp images
    Keep only the normalized output dict in memory

  STEP 10 — RETURN
    Return normalized dict + confidence score + doc_type

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1.2 OCR TRIGGER RULES (WHEN TO RUN OCR)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TRIGGER — RUN OCR:
    ✅ User uploads a new file
    ✅ User replaces an existing file
    ✅ User recaptures a photo
    ✅ User taps an explicit "Rescan" button

  DO NOT TRIGGER OCR:
    ❌ User types in a text field
    ❌ User moves to the next step (if file unchanged)
    ❌ User taps the back button
    ❌ App resumes from background (use cached result)

  OCR RESULT CACHING:
    After OCR runs on a file, store result in temp cache with file hash.
    If same file uploaded again (same hash), use cached result, skip OCR.

================================================================================
SECTION 2 — DOCUMENT TYPE DETECTION
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  After raw OCR text is extracted, run document_type_detect() to identify
  which parser to use. Detection is keyword-based on the full OCR text.

  def document_type_detect(raw_text: str) -> str:
    text = raw_text.upper()

    # Aadhaar detection
    if "AADHAAR" in text or "UNIQUE IDENTIFICATION" in text or "UIDAI" in text:
        if "FRONT" clue or photo area detected:
            return "AADHAAR_FRONT"
        return "AADHAAR_BACK"

    # PAN detection
    if "INCOME TAX DEPARTMENT" in text or "PERMANENT ACCOUNT NUMBER" in text:
        return "PAN_CARD"

    # Bank statement detection — detect which bank
    if "AXIS BANK" in text:
        return "BANK_STMT_AXIS"
    if "CANARA BANK" in text:
        return "BANK_STMT_CANARA"
    if "STATE BANK OF INDIA" in text or "SBI" in text:
        return "BANK_STMT_SBI"
    if "HDFC BANK" in text:
        return "BANK_STMT_HDFC"
    if "ICICI BANK" in text:
        return "BANK_STMT_ICICI"
    if any keyword in text for ["ACCOUNT STATEMENT", "TRANSACTION DATE",
                                 "DEBIT", "CREDIT", "BALANCE"]:
        return "BANK_STMT_GENERIC"

    # Utility bills
    if "TAMIL NADU ELECTRICITY" in text or "TNEB" in text or "TANGEDCO" in text:
        return "BILL_ELECTRICITY_TNEB"
    if "BESCOM" in text or "BANGALORE ELECTRICITY" in text:
        return "BILL_ELECTRICITY_BESCOM"
    if "INDANE" in text or "INDIAN OIL" in text:
        return "BILL_LPG_INDANE"
    if "HP GAS" in text or "HINDUSTAN PETROLEUM" in text:
        return "BILL_LPG_HP"
    if "BHARAT GAS" in text or "BPCL" in text:
        return "BILL_LPG_BHARAT"
    if "JIO" in text and ("POSTPAID" in text or "BILL" in text):
        return "BILL_MOBILE_JIO"
    if "AIRTEL" in text and "BILL" in text:
        return "BILL_MOBILE_AIRTEL"

    # Vehicle documents
    if "REGISTRATION CERTIFICATE" in text or "RC BOOK" in text:
        return "RC_BOOK"
    if "DRIVING LICENCE" in text or "DRIVING LICENSE" in text:
        return "DRIVING_LICENCE"

    # Platform earnings screenshots
    if "SWIGGY" in text and ("EARNINGS" in text or "TRIPS" in text):
        return "EARNINGS_SWIGGY"
    if "ZOMATO" in text and "EARNINGS" in text:
        return "EARNINGS_ZOMATO"
    if "OLA" in text and "EARNINGS" in text:
        return "EARNINGS_OLA"
    if "UBER" in text and "EARNINGS" in text:
        return "EARNINGS_UBER"

    # Government scheme cards
    if "E-SHRAM" in text or "ESHRAM" in text or "UNORGANISED WORKER" in text:
        return "ESHRAM_CARD"

    # Insurance
    if "HEALTH INSURANCE" in text or "MEDICLAIM" in text:
        return "INSURANCE_HEALTH"
    if "MOTOR INSURANCE" in text or "VEHICLE INSURANCE" in text:
        return "INSURANCE_VEHICLE"
    if "LIFE INSURANCE" in text or "LIC" in text or "PREMIUM RECEIPT" in text:
        return "INSURANCE_LIFE"

    # ITR
    if "INCOME TAX RETURN" in text or "ACKNOWLEDGEMENT" in text and "ITR" in text:
        return "ITR_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT"

    return "UNKNOWN"

================================================================================
SECTION 3 — PARSERS PER DOCUMENT TYPE
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  For each document type below, look at the sample file in /inputs/
  and generate a parser function that:
    1. Takes raw OCR text (string) as input
    2. Extracts the target fields listed
    3. Returns a dict matching the normalized schema in Section 5
    4. Handles common OCR errors (O vs 0, I vs 1, missing spaces)
    5. Uses the regex patterns provided as starting points — improve based
       on actual sample files

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.1 AADHAAR FRONT (step-2/aadhaar_front_sample.jpg)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - aadhaar_number   (12-digit, format: XXXX XXXX XXXX)
    - full_name        (line above DOB)
    - date_of_birth    (DD/MM/YYYY)
    - gender           (Male / Female / Transgender)
    - year_of_birth    (fallback if full DOB not found)

  PARSER STRATEGY:
    - Aadhaar numbers are always 12 digits in groups of 4
    - Name is in bold, typically 1–2 lines above DOB
    - DOB is prefixed by "DOB:" or "Date of Birth:" or "जन्म तिथि:"
    - Gender is prefixed by "Male" / "Female" or in Hindi/Tamil

  STARTING REGEX:
    aadhaar_pattern = r'\d{4}\s\d{4}\s\d{4}'
    dob_pattern     = r'DOB[:\s]*([0-9]{2}/[0-9]{2}/[0-9]{4})'
    yob_pattern     = r'Year of Birth[:\s]*(\d{4})'
    gender_pattern  = r'\b(Male|Female|Transgender)\b'

  OCR ERROR HANDLING:
    - Aadhaar may OCR as "1234 5O67 89I2" — replace O→0, I→1
    - Name may have extra spaces — use strip() and re.sub(r'\s+', ' ', name)
    - DOB separators may OCR as '-' or '.' instead of '/' — normalize

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-2/aadhaar_front_sample.jpg
  IMPROVE REGEX based on actual layout observed in the sample.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.2 AADHAAR BACK (step-2/aadhaar_back_sample.jpg)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - address_line1
    - address_line2
    - district
    - state
    - pin_code

  PARSER STRATEGY:
    - Address is a multi-line block below the QR code
    - State name appears before PIN code
    - PIN code is always 6 digits

  STARTING REGEX:
    pin_pattern   = r'\b([1-9][0-9]{5})\b'
    state_pattern = r'(Tamil Nadu|Maharashtra|Karnataka|...) all states list

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-2/aadhaar_back_sample.jpg

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.3 PAN CARD (step-2/pan_sample.jpg)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - pan_number        (format: ABCDE1234F)
    - full_name         (name of PAN holder)
    - father_name       (father's name on PAN)
    - date_of_birth     (DD/MM/YYYY)

  PARSER STRATEGY:
    - PAN number is always 5 letters + 4 digits + 1 letter, uppercase
    - Full name appears in CAPITAL LETTERS on the card
    - Father's name appears below the full name
    - DOB is in DD/MM/YYYY format

  STARTING REGEX:
    pan_pattern  = r'[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1}'
    dob_pattern  = r'([0-9]{2}/[0-9]{2}/[0-9]{4})'
    name_pattern = r'^([A-Z\s]+)$'  (all-caps line)

  OCR ERROR HANDLING:
    - PAN may OCR as "ABCDE 1234F" (extra space) — remove spaces in PAN field
    - Letters O and 0 confusion in PAN number — apply domain logic
      (4th character of PAN is always P/C/H/F/A/T/B/L/J/G — use this to validate)

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-2/pan_sample.jpg

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.4 BANK STATEMENTS — AXIS BANK (step-3/axis_bank_statement.pdf)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS PER TRANSACTION:
    - date          (DD/MM/YYYY or DD-MM-YYYY or DD MMM YYYY)
    - narration     (description / transaction name)
    - debit_amount  (float, empty if credit transaction)
    - credit_amount (float, empty if debit transaction)
    - balance       (float, closing balance after transaction)

  ACCOUNT HEADER FIELDS:
    - account_number
    - account_holder_name
    - statement_from_date
    - statement_to_date
    - ifsc_code

  PARSER STRATEGY FOR AXIS BANK:
    Step 1: Use pdfplumber to extract text — Axis Bank statements are
            digital PDFs (not scanned), so text extraction is direct.
    Step 2: Find the transaction table. Look for the header row containing
            keywords: "Date", "Narration", "Chq./Ref.No.", "Value Dt",
            "Withdrawal Amt.", "Deposit Amt.", "Closing Balance"
    Step 3: Extract rows below the header.
    Step 4: Handle multi-line narrations (narration text wraps to next line
            in Axis statements — detect by checking if first column is empty).
    Step 5: Parse amounts — Axis uses comma-separated format (1,23,456.78)
            Remove commas, convert to float.

  AXIS BANK COLUMN POSITIONS (approximate, verify with sample):
    Col 0: Date (DD/MM/YYYY)
    Col 1: Narration (multi-line possible)
    Col 2: Cheque/Ref No
    Col 3: Value Date
    Col 4: Withdrawal Amount (debit)
    Col 5: Deposit Amount (credit)
    Col 6: Closing Balance

  STARTING CODE:
    import pdfplumber
    def parse_axis_statement(pdf_path):
        transactions = []
        with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
            for page in pdf.pages:
                tables = page.extract_tables()
                for table in tables:
                    for row in table:
                        # parse row into transaction dict
        return transactions

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-3/axis_bank_statement.pdf
  ADJUST column indices and header detection based on actual sample.

3.5 BANK STATEMENTS — CANARA BANK (step-3/canara_bank_statement.pdf)

  TARGET FIELDS: Same as 3.4

  PARSER STRATEGY FOR CANARA BANK:
    Canara Bank statements may be digital or printed-then-scanned.
    Step 1: Try pdfplumber text extraction.
    Step 2: If text extraction fails → convert PDF pages to images → OCR.
    Step 3: Canara header row keywords: "Tran Date", "Particulars",
            "Debit", "Credit", "Balance"
    Step 4: Date format is DD/MM/YYYY.
    Step 5: Canara narrations include NEFT/UPI/ATW/CHQ prefixes — preserve them.

  CANARA BANK COLUMN POSITIONS (verify with sample):
    Col 0: Transaction Date
    Col 1: Value Date
    Col 2: Particulars (narration)
    Col 3: Debit
    Col 4: Credit
    Col 5: Balance

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-3/canara_bank_statement.pdf

3.6 BANK STATEMENTS — SBI (step-3/sbi_bank_statement.pdf)

  TARGET FIELDS: Same as 3.4

  PARSER STRATEGY FOR SBI:
    SBI statements downloaded from YONO/NetBanking are digital PDFs.
    SBI header row keywords: "Txn Date", "Value Date", "Description",
    "Ref No./Cheque No.", "Debit", "Credit", "Balance"
    SBI uses format: "DD MMM YYYY" (e.g., 01 Apr 2026)
    Parse date using: datetime.strptime(date_str, "%d %b %Y")

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-3/sbi_bank_statement.pdf

3.7 BANK STATEMENTS — HDFC (step-3/hdfc_bank_statement.pdf)

  TARGET FIELDS: Same as 3.4

  PARSER STRATEGY FOR HDFC:
    HDFC header keywords: "Date", "Narration", "Value Dt", "Debit Amount",
    "Credit Amount", "Chq/Ref Number", "Closing Balance"
    HDFC narrations are long and structured (UPI/NEFT/IMPS prefix included).
    HDFC date: DD/MM/YY format — convert to DD/MM/YYYY (add 20 prefix to YY).

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-3/hdfc_bank_statement.pdf

3.8 BANK STATEMENTS — ICICI (step-3/icici_bank_statement.pdf)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS: Same as 3.4

  PARSER STRATEGY FOR ICICI:
    ICICI header keywords: "S.No.", "Value Date", "Transaction Date",
    "Cheque Number", "Transaction Remarks", "Withdrawal Amount (INR)",
    "Deposit Amount (INR)", "Balance (INR)"
    ICICI amounts include trailing spaces — strip before converting to float.

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-3/icici_bank_statement.pdf

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.9 BANK STATEMENTS — GENERIC FALLBACK (any unknown bank)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS: Same as 3.4

  PARSER STRATEGY — GENERIC TABLE PARSER:
    This parser handles any bank not matched by the above 5.
    Strategy: find the transaction table by searching for a row that contains
    ALL of these keywords in some combination:
      "date", "debit", "credit", "balance"
    Once header row found, extract all rows below it.
    Use column position of "debit" and "credit" keywords to map amounts.
    Any numeric value in "debit" column → debit_amount
    Any numeric value in "credit" column → credit_amount
    Last numeric column → balance

  CONFIDENCE SCORING:
    Generic parser confidence = (successfully_parsed_rows / total_rows)
    If confidence < 0.70 → flag as NEEDS_REVIEW

  THIS PARSER IS THE FALLBACK — always runs last, after all specific parsers fail.

3.10 UTILITY BILLS — ELECTRICITY — TNEB/TANGEDCO
         (step-4/tneb_electricity_bill.jpg)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - consumer_number   (unique identifier, must be same across 6 months)
    - bill_month        (Month Year, e.g. "March 2026")
    - bill_amount       (total amount due, float)
    - due_date          (DD/MM/YYYY)
    - payment_status    (Paid / Unpaid)
    - units_consumed    (integer)

  PARSER STRATEGY:
    TNEB bills are printed images (not digital PDFs).
    Use image OCR pipeline.
    Look for:
      - "Consumer No" or "SC No" → consumer number
      - "Bill Month" or "Billing Period" → month
      - "Amount Payable" or "Total Amount Due" → bill amount
      - "Due Date" or "Last Date" → due date
      - "Units Consumed" or "Consumption" → units

  STARTING REGEX:
    consumer_pattern = r'(?:Consumer No|SC No)[:\s]*([A-Z0-9]+)'
    amount_pattern   = r'(?:Amount Payable|Total Due)[:\s]*([\d,]+\.?\d*)'
    due_date_pattern = r'(?:Due Date|Last Date)[:\s]*(\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4})'
    units_pattern    = r'(?:Units Consumed|Consumption)[:\s]*(\d+)'

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-4/tneb_electricity_bill.jpg

    Look for:
      - Registration number: always in format STATE_CODE + DIGITS + LETTERS + DIGITS
        Regex: r'[A-Z]{2}[\s-]?[0-9]{1,2}[\s-]?[A-Z]{1,3}[\s-]?[0-9]{1,4}'
      - "Name of Owner" or "Registered Owner"
      - "Vehicle Class" or "Class of Vehicle"
      - "Chassis No" → chassis
      - "Engine No" → engine
      - "Registration Date" or "Date of Registration"
      - "Registration Validity" or "Valid Upto" → expiry

    Vehicle classes appear as codes: MC, LMV, MCWG, TRANS

3.15 PLATFORM EARNINGS — SWIGGY (step-5/swiggy_earnings_screenshot.png)
     PLATFORM EARNINGS — ZOMATO (step-5/zomato_earnings_screenshot.png)
     PLATFORM EARNINGS — OLA / UBER (step-5/ola_earnings_screenshot.png)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - platform_name         (Swiggy / Zomato / Ola / Uber)
    - month_label           (e.g. "April 2026")
    - total_earnings        (float in ₹)
    - total_trips           (integer)
    - account_name          (name shown on earnings page)

  PARSER STRATEGY:
    Screenshots have NO fixed layout — use entity extraction, not table parser.

    Entity extraction approach:
    Step 1: OCR full screenshot
    Step 2: Find total earnings → scan for largest ₹ amount on the screen
            Regex: r'[₹Rs\.]+\s*([\d,]+)'
    Step 3: Find total trips → look for pattern: NUMBER + "trips" / "deliveries" / "rides"
            Regex: r'(\d+)\s*(?:trips|deliveries|rides|orders)'
    Step 4: Find month → look for Month + Year pattern
            Regex: r'(January|February|March|April|May|June|July|August|
                       September|October|November|December)\s+\d{4}'
    Step 5: Platform confirmed by logo keyword already detected in document_type_detect()

  NOTE: Do NOT try to parse exact UI layout. Screenshots vary by app version.
  Use pure text entity extraction for all platform screenshots.

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-5/swiggy_earnings_screenshot.png
           inputs/step-5/zomato_earnings_screenshot.png

3.17 INSURANCE — HEALTH POLICY (step-7/health_insurance_policy.pdf)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - policy_number
    - policy_holder_name
    - insurer_name          (Star Health / Niva Bupa / Care etc.)
    - sum_insured           (float)
    - annual_premium        (float)
    - policy_start_date     (DD/MM/YYYY)
    - policy_expiry_date    (DD/MM/YYYY)

  PARSER STRATEGY:
    Health insurance policies are digital PDFs.
    Use pdfplumber text extraction.
    Look for:
      - "Policy No" or "Policy Number" → policy_number
      - "Insured Name" or "Name of Insured" → holder_name
      - "Sum Insured" or "Coverage Amount" → sum_insured
      - "Premium" or "Annual Premium" → premium
      - "Policy Period" or "Valid From...To" → start + expiry dates
    Policy expiry must be > today's date (validate this in parser output)

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-7/health_insurance_policy.pdf

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.18 INSURANCE — VEHICLE (step-7/vehicle_insurance_cert.jpg)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - policy_number
    - policy_holder_name
    - vehicle_number        (must match RC book vehicle number from Step 5)
    - insurer_name
    - policy_expiry_date    (DD/MM/YYYY)

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-7/vehicle_insurance_cert.jpg

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.19 INSURANCE — LIFE INSURANCE (step-7/life_insurance_bond.pdf)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  TARGET FIELDS:
    - policy_number
    - policy_holder_name
    - insurer_name          (LIC / HDFC Life / SBI Life etc.)
    - sum_assured           (float)
    - annual_premium        (float)
    - policy_start_date
    - policy_expiry_date
    - coverage_status       (Active / Lapsed)

  LOOK AT: inputs/step-7/life_insurance_bond.pdf

================================================================================
SECTION 4 — COMMON OCR ERROR CORRECTIONS
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  Apply these corrections to ALL raw OCR output before running field parsers.

  def clean_ocr_text(text: str) -> str:
    # Fix common OCR letter/number confusions
    # Only apply in NUMERIC-EXPECTED contexts
    text = text.strip()

    # In Aadhaar/PAN number fields only:
    def fix_number_field(s):
        return s.replace('O', '0').replace('I', '1').replace('l', '1')
              .replace('S', '5').replace('B', '8')

    # Normalize whitespace
    text = re.sub(r'\s+', ' ', text)

    # Normalize date separators
    text = re.sub(r'(\d{2})[-\.](\d{2})[-\.](\d{4})', r'\1/\2/\3', text)

    # Normalize currency symbols
    text = text.replace('Rs.', '₹').replace('INR', '₹')

    # Normalize amount commas (keep only decimals)
    def parse_amount(s):
        return float(s.replace(',', '').replace('₹', '').strip())

    return text

  FUZZY NAME MATCHING:
    When comparing OCR-extracted name with API-returned name:
    Use rapidfuzz.fuzz.token_sort_ratio()
    Threshold: ≥ 85 → MATCH, < 85 → MISMATCH

    from rapidfuzz import fuzz
    def names_match(name1: str, name2: str) -> bool:
        score = fuzz.token_sort_ratio(name1.upper(), name2.upper())
        return score >= 85

================================================================================
SECTION 5 — NORMALIZED OUTPUT SCHEMAS
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  Every parser MUST return a dict conforming to these schemas exactly.
  Downstream feature engineering reads these schemas.
  Never add extra fields. Never rename fields.
  Use None (Python) / null (JSON) for missing optional fields.

SCHEMA 1 — IDENTITY DOCUMENT:
{
    "doc_type": str,            // "AADHAAR_FRONT" | "PAN_CARD" etc.
    "full_name": str or None,
    "date_of_birth": str or None,  // "DD/MM/YYYY" format always
    "id_number": str or None,       // Aadhaar or PAN number
    "address": str or None,         // Aadhaar back only
    "state": str or None,
    "pin_code": str or None,
    "confidence": float             // 0.0–1.0
}

SCHEMA 2 — BANK TRANSACTION (one per transaction):
{
    "date": str,                // "YYYY-MM-DD" always (convert all formats)
    "narration": str,
    "debit_amount": float or None,
    "credit_amount": float or None,
    "balance": float or None,
    "confidence": float
}

SCHEMA 3 — BANK STATEMENT HEADER:
{
    "doc_type": str,             // "BANK_STMT_AXIS" etc.
    "bank_name": str,
    "account_number": str or None,
    "account_holder_name": str or None,
    "ifsc_code": str or None,
    "statement_from": str or None,   // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "statement_to": str or None,     // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "transactions": list,            // list of SCHEMA 2 dicts
    "confidence": float
}

SCHEMA 4 — UTILITY BILL:
{
    "doc_type": str,             // "BILL_ELECTRICITY_TNEB" etc.
    "provider_name": str,
    "consumer_number": str or None,
    "mobile_number": str or None,  // mobile bills only
    "bill_month": str or None,     // "YYYY-MM" always
    "bill_amount": float or None,
    "due_date": str or None,       // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "payment_status": str or None, // "Paid" | "Unpaid" | "Unknown"
    "units_consumed": int or None,  // electricity only
    "confidence": float
}

SCHEMA 5 — VEHICLE DOCUMENT:
{
    "doc_type": str,             // "RC_BOOK" | "DRIVING_LICENCE"
    "vehicle_number": str or None,
    "owner_name": str or None,
    "vehicle_class": str or None,
    "chassis_number": str or None,
    "engine_number": str or None,
    "registration_date": str or None,  // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "expiry_date": str or None,        // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "dl_number": str or None,          // DL only
    "vehicle_classes_authorized": list or None,  // DL only
    "confidence": float
}

SCHEMA 6 — PLATFORM EARNINGS:
{
    "doc_type": str,             // "EARNINGS_SWIGGY" etc.
    "platform_name": str,
    "month_label": str or None,  // "YYYY-MM"
    "total_earnings": float or None,
    "total_trips": int or None,
    "account_name": str or None,
    "confidence": float
}

SCHEMA 7 — INSURANCE DOCUMENT:
{
    "doc_type": str,             // "INSURANCE_HEALTH" etc.
    "policy_number": str or None,
    "policy_holder_name": str or None,
    "insurer_name": str or None,
    "sum_insured": float or None,
    "annual_premium": float or None,
    "policy_start_date": str or None,  // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "policy_expiry_date": str or None, // "YYYY-MM-DD"
    "coverage_status": str or None,    // "Active" | "Lapsed" | "Expired"
    "vehicle_number": str or None,     // vehicle insurance only
    "confidence": float
}

SECTION 6 — VALIDATION RULES (POST-PARSER)
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  After any parser returns a normalized schema, run these validations.
  These are NOT field-format validations (regex) — these are BUSINESS RULE
  validations. Flag any failure as a ValidationResult.

  AADHAAR:
    ✅ id_number must be exactly 12 digits (after removing spaces)
    ✅ date_of_birth must be a valid past date (> 18 years ago)
    ✅ full_name must be present and ≥ 3 characters

  PAN:
    ✅ id_number must match [A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1} exactly
    ✅ 4th character of PAN must be P/C/H/F/A/T/B/L/J/G (person type)
    ✅ full_name must match Aadhaar name (fuzzy ≥ 85%)

  BANK STATEMENT:
    ✅ Must have ≥ 30 transactions
    ✅ statement_from must be ≥ 6 months before statement_to
    ✅ All debit_amount and credit_amount must be positive floats
    ✅ balance must be non-negative
    ✅ account_holder_name must match Aadhaar name (fuzzy ≥ 85%)

  UTILITY BILLS:
    ✅ consumer_number must be identical across all 6 bills of same type
    ✅ bill_month must cover 6 consecutive months
    ✅ bill_amount must be positive float
    ✅ Mobile bill: mobile_number must match Step 1 mobile number

  VEHICLE DOCUMENTS:
    ✅ vehicle_number format: [A-Z]{2}[0-9]{2}[A-Z]{1,3}[0-9]{1,4}
    ✅ RC expiry_date must be > today
    ✅ owner_name must match Aadhaar name (fuzzy ≥ 85%)
    ✅ DL vehicle_classes_authorized must contain the RC vehicle_class

  INSURANCE:
    ✅ policy_expiry_date must be > today
    ✅ policy_holder_name must match Aadhaar name (fuzzy ≥ 85%)
    ✅ Vehicle insurance: vehicle_number must match RC book vehicle_number
    ✅ sum_insured must be positive float

================================================================================
SECTION 7 — OCR DATA LIFECYCLE (WHERE DATA LIVES)
================================================================================

  STAGE 1 — UPLOAD EVENT
    Raw file: stored in app private cache /cache/uploads/{uuid}/
    Access: only the app process can read this

  STAGE 2 — DURING OCR
    Preprocessed image: temp file in /cache/ocr_temp/{uuid}/
    Raw OCR text: held in RAM only (never written to disk)
    Parser output: held in RAM only

  STAGE 3 — AFTER SUCCESSFUL PARSING + VALIDATION
    Normalized schema: saved to encrypted local DB (Step-specific table)
    Example: step2_identity table stores SCHEMA 1 output
    Raw file: DELETED immediately after parser succeeds
    OCR temp files: DELETED immediately
    Raw OCR text: cleared from RAM

  STAGE 4 — IF VALIDATION FAILS
    Parser output: held in RAM briefly for error display
    Raw file: kept in /cache/uploads/{uuid}/ for retry
    User shown: which fields failed, asked to re-upload
    On re-upload: old file deleted, new file processed

  STAGE 5 — AFTER ALL 9 STEPS COMPLETE (POST-SCORING)
    All normalized schemas in encrypted DB: DELETED
    Only FinalReportObject retained (encrypted)

  STORAGE SUMMARY:
    Type                        | Where              | Duration
    ----------------------------|--------------------|------------------
    Raw uploaded file           | cache/uploads/     | Until parser done
    OCR preprocessed image      | cache/ocr_temp/    | During OCR only
    Raw OCR text string         | RAM only           | During parsing only
    Normalized schema dict      | Encrypted local DB | Until scoring done
    FinalReportObject           | Encrypted local DB | Long term
    Any log of raw OCR text     | NOWHERE            | Never stored

SECTION 8 — PYTHON TEST HARNESS STRUCTURE
================================================================================

AI INSTRUCTION:
  Generate a Python test harness with this structure:

  /gigcredit-ocr/
    ├── ocr_engine.py           ← main OCR + preprocessing functions
    ├── document_detector.py    ← document_type_detect() function
    ├── parsers/
    │   ├── __init__.py
    │   ├── identity_parsers.py   ← Aadhaar + PAN parsers
    │   ├── bank_parsers.py       ← All bank statement parsers + generic
    │   ├── bill_parsers.py       ← All utility bill parsers
    │   ├── vehicle_parsers.py    ← RC book + DL parsers
    │   ├── earnings_parsers.py   ← Platform earnings screenshot parsers
    │   ├── govt_parsers.py       ← eShram, ITR parsers
    │   └── insurance_parsers.py  ← Health, Vehicle, Life insurance parsers
    ├── validators.py             ← All validation rules from Section 6
    ├── normalizer.py             ← Output schema normalization
    ├── test_runner.py            ← Run all parsers against sample inputs
    └── schema/
        └── normalized_schemas.json

  TEST RUNNER LOGIC (test_runner.py):

    import os
    from ocr_engine import process_file
    from validators import validate_output

    def run_all_tests():
        input_dir = "./inputs"
        results = []

        for step_folder in sorted(os.listdir(input_dir)):
            step_path = os.path.join(input_dir, step_folder)
            for filename in os.listdir(step_path):
                file_path = os.path.join(step_path, filename)
                result = process_file(file_path)
                validation = validate_output(result)
                results.append({
                    "file": filename,
                    "step": step_folder,
                    "doc_type": result.get("doc_type"),
                    "confidence": result.get("confidence"),
                    "validation_passed": validation["passed"],
                    "fields_found": [k for k,v in result.items() if v is not None]
                })

        # Print summary
        for r in results:
            status = "✅ PASS" if r["validation_passed"] else "❌ FAIL"
            print(f"{status} | {r['step']} | {r['file']} | conf={r['confidence']:.2f}")

    if __name__ == "__main__":
        run_all_tests()

GIGCREDIT — SHAP OUTPUT TO LLM RESPONSE TO REPORT TEMPLATE
PIPELINE SPECIFICATION
VERSION 1.0
STATUS: PLANNING SPECIFICATION — NOT FINAL IMPLEMENTATION
================================================================================

SCOPE OF THIS DOCUMENT:
  This specification covers ONLY the pipeline from:
    SHAP Lookup Output
      → LLM Payload Construction
        → Backend Groq API Call
          → LLM Response Processing
            → Report Template Assembly

  This document does NOT cover:
    - Feature engineering (see Feature Engineering Specification)
    - Evaluation engine / pillar model training (see ML Specification)
    - Meta-learner architecture (see ML Specification)
    - Confidence engine logic (see ML Specification)
    - Verification API layer (see Backend Server Specification)
    - Final report PDF export (see Report Export Specification)
    - Loan matching engine (see Loan Matching Specification)

IMPORTANT CORRECTIONS FROM PREVIOUS DRAFTS:
  ✅ 7 pillars only (P8 Tax Compliance does NOT exist in GigCredit)
  ✅ Confidence engine logic belongs entirely to ML pipeline (meta-learner)
  ✅ Report template has NO risk level field
  ✅ LLM returns ONLY explanation text + suggestions (nothing else)
  ✅ Score + pillar breakdown go DIRECTLY from evaluation engine to report
  ✅ SHAP factors go to BOTH report template (raw) AND LLM payload (as context)
  ✅ Top 3 positive + Top 3 negative factors (not top 5)

================================================================================
SECTION 1 — PIPELINE OVERVIEW
================================================================================

This pipeline has two parallel tracks and one backend track.

TRACK A — DIRECT TO REPORT (No LLM):
  Evaluation Engine Output → Report Template Sections 1 and 2
  SHAP Lookup Output → Report Template Section 3 (raw factors only)

TRACK B — VIA LLM (Backend Required):
  SHAP Lookup Output + Score Context → LLM Payload
  LLM Payload → Backend → Groq API
  Groq Response → Report Template Section 3 (explanation text)
                → Report Template Section 4 (suggestions)

TRACK C — LOAN RECOMMENDATIONS (Matching Engine, separate):
  Evaluation Engine Output → Matching Engine → Report Template Section 5

BOTH TRACKS RUN CONCURRENTLY:
  Track A populates sections 1, 2, and 3 (raw) immediately on-device.
  Track B runs in parallel — app shows loading indicator for sections 3
  (explanation text) and 4 while waiting for Groq response.
  If Track B fails → fallback text fills sections 3 and 4.
  User is never blocked from seeing their score and pillar breakdown.

================================================================================
SECTION 2 — WHAT THIS PIPELINE RECEIVES (INPUTS)
================================================================================

This pipeline starts AFTER the following have completed on-device:

  INPUT 1 — EVALUATION ENGINE FINAL OUTPUT
  ─────────────────────────────────────────
  Produced by: meta-learner (Logistic Regression, pure Dart)
  Contains:
    {
      "final_score": 682,
      "grade": "B",
      "pillar_scores": {
        "income_stability": 72,
        "payment_discipline": 68,
        "debt_management": 55,
        "savings_behaviour": 61,
        "work_identity": 78,
        "financial_resilience": 45,
        "social_accountability": 60
      },
      "confidence_level": "High"
    }

  NOTE ON CONFIDENCE LEVEL:
    confidence_level is computed by the meta-learner as part of ML logic.
    It reflects data quality across all 9 steps (missing docs, fallback
    values, unverified fields reduce confidence).
    It is an OUTPUT of the ML pipeline — NOT a separate engine in the
    report pipeline. The report pipeline only READS it, never modifies it.
    Values: "High" | "Medium" | "Low"

  NOTE ON RISK LEVEL:
    There is NO risk level in the report template.
    The grade (S/A/B/C/D/E) is sufficient.
    Risk level is NOT displayed to the user.

  INPUT 2 — SHAP LOOKUP OUTPUT
  ─────────────────────────────
  Produced by: on-device SHAP lookup engine (reads shap_lookup.json)
  Contains:
    {
      "positive_factors": [
        {

      "language": "Tamil"
